## Q2469. Strength of inhomogeneous ring regularity

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3) · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

What is the reverse-mathematical strength over RCA₀ of the assertion: for every commutative ring R, finite m×n matrix A over R and nonzero b∈R^m, if Ax=b has no constant solution, some finite coloring of R has no monochromatic solution?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2469: Gabriela Laboska. PhD, University of Chicago, 2025. Advisors: Maryanthe Malliaris; Denis Hirschfeldt. Origin for question 2469: Leader–Russell 2020; effective questions Laboska 2025. Setup for question 2469: A monochromatic solution has all coordinates the same color; a constant solution has all coordinates equal. Rings and algebraic operations are countable coded structures.

**Source.** Gabriela Laboska. *On the Effectiveness of Partition Regularity over Algebraic Structures*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/jsl.2025.10178) Location: Open Question 3, p. 5; §2.1.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2469, checked 7 October 2026: Checked 7 October 2026: question retained in the reviewed source; targeted searches found no matching resolution.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: WKL0 proves the retained ring-coloring assertion over RCA0, with at most [3(n-1)]^(n-1) colors for n>=2 and one color for n<=1. A finite-presentation construction avoids comprehension of the range sR; supplied finite integer matrices are diagonalized by an explicit primitive-recursive algorithm. Every PA degree relative to the input can compute a witnessing coloring. Restricted scope: Countable coded commutative rings, as required for the retained second-order arithmetic statement; no additional ring hypothesis. Only an upper bound on logical strength is proved. Remaining: No WKL0 reversal, lower bound on the optimal color count, or exact reverse-mathematical classification is proved. Recorded from the external B2 return  (backfilled 2026-10-08); selected exact checks in the intake; verification archive not uploaded; not independently re-derived here. [Details](../../solutions/b2-2026-10-07/B2_Research_Report_2026-10-07.md).
- **Related result** (B2-R5: auxiliary proof submission): Over RCA0, WKL0 implies the ring-coloring assertion, with q(n)=[3(n-1)]^(n-1) colors for n>=2. The finite feasibility lemma is proved by explicit integer-matrix diagonalization. Location: Part E. Restricted scope: Only the upper logical bound is proved. Full retained question(s) remain unresolved; consult intake checks and archive limits. [Report](../../solutions/b2-2026-10-07/README.md).
- **Partial result** (B2-CONTINUATION-RCA0-SINGLE-DIFFERENCE: RCA0 three-color single-difference avoidance): For every coded countable abelian group and nonzero b, the translation graph of x-y=b has degree at most two. Greedy coloring in the enumeration gives a three-color avoidance proof in RCA0. Remaining/limits: This is a single-equation subcase. It does not classify the ring/matrix assertion or imply a two-color reversal. Prior partial mark retained. [Report](../../solutions/b2-continuation-2026-10-12/arithmetic/RAMSEY-ATTEMPTS.md).
- **Investigated, unresolved** (B2-CONTINUATION-STOP-REGISTER: Every B2 target reaches its own stopping condition): All 81 catalog questions have five reviewed substantive failed full-target routes (405 total); A-TRANSFER is solved locally. Partial proofs do not reset counters. The same retained agent completed ten catalog packets and the broader proof; root completed the other 71 catalog questions and the final filing. Remaining/limits: The catalog questions remain unresolved. Source, shell and service failures are excluded; new finite checks do not recover the original missing archive. [Report](../../solutions/b2-continuation-2026-10-12/STATUS.json).

**Further links.** [1](https://knowledge.uchicago.edu/records/7rfhc-ymn78)

## Q2470. Maximal complexity of hyperfiniteness

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.2.3) · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

Is the set of Borel codes for hyperfinite CBERs on Baire space Σ¹₂-complete?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2470, 2471: Cecelia Higgins. PhD, University of California, Los Angeles, 2025. Advisors: Andrew Marks; Anton Bernshteyn. Origin for questions 2470, 2471: Dougherty–Jackson–Kechris 1994; Weiss 1984. Setup for questions 2470, 2471: A CBER is a countable Borel equivalence relation on a standard Borel space. It is hyperfinite if it is an increasing countable union of Borel equivalence relations with finite classes.

**Source.** Cecelia Higgins. *Measurable Brooks’s Theorem for Directed Graphs and the Complexity of Finite Borel Asymptotic Dimension*. University of California, Los Angeles, 2025. Advisor(s): Andrew Marks; Anton Bernshteyn. [primary source](https://escholarship.org/uc/item/7w93q45v) Location: Question 3.2.3, p. 54.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2470, 2471, checked 7 October 2026: Checked 7 October 2026: question retained in the reviewed source; targeted searches found no matching resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1017/fms.2026.10180) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18439v2)

## Q2472. Bounded-degree dimension complexity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.1) · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

Is the set of codes for bounded-degree Borel graphs with finite Borel asymptotic dimension Σ¹₂-complete?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2472: see question 2470. Origin for question 2472: Preprint 2024; Question 6.1. Setup for question 2472: For a Borel graph G, asdim_B(G)≤d means: for every integer r≥1 there is a Borel partition into d+1 sets whose components in the distance≤r graph have uniformly bounded G-diameter.

**Source.** Jan Grebík; Cecelia Higgins. *Complexity of Finite Borel Asymptotic Dimension*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/fms.2026.10180) Location: Question 6.1, p. 15.

## Q2474. Hypersmoothness for commuting functions

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.4) · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

For every Borel action of a countable commutative monoid M on a standard Borel space X, is the equivalence relation generated by x∼mx hypersmooth?

**Context.** Origin for question 2474: Folklore Question 1.4; Question 5.17. Setup for question 2474: Hypersmooth means an increasing countable union of smooth Borel equivalence relations. Smooth means Borel reducible to equality on a standard Borel space.

**Source.** Forte Shinko; Felix Weilacher; Jing Yu. *Hyperfiniteness of bounded-to-one actions of commutative monoids*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18439v2) Location: Question 1.4, p. 2.

## Q2475. Subcountability forces anti-complements

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.14) · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

Does CZF plus Subcountability prove ∀x[(∃y∀z(¬¬(z∈x)→z∈y))→x⊆1]?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2475: Hanul Jeon. Proof theory for higher pointclasses. PhD, Cornell University, 2026. Advisors: Justin Moore; James Walsh. Origin for question 2475: Question 3.14. Setup for question 2475: CZF is constructive Zermelo–Fraenkel set theory. Subcountability says every set is an image of a subset of ω; 1={∅}.

**Source.** Hanul Jeon; James E. Hanson. *The Axiom of Double Complement and its opposites*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00861v2) Location: Question 3.14 and Definition 3.1, pp. 7–10.

<a id="q2567"></a>

## Q2567. Lower factors of wim extensions

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.32) · **Collection** 26

If E≺E₁ is wim-constructible and E≺E₀≺E₁, must E₀/E be wim-constructible?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2567, 2568: Pietro Freni. Structural Investigations in some Classes of o-minimal Fields. PhD, University of Leeds, 2024. Advisors: Vincenzo Mantova; H. Dugald Macpherson. Origin for questions 2567, 2568: Thesis Question 3.2.28(1),(2), printed p. 141. Setup for questions 2567, 2568: Fix a complete o-minimal theory T of ordered fields. Models (E,O) of T_convex have a proper convex valuation ring closed under continuous parameter-free T-definable functions. All extensions below are elementary T_convex extensions with compatible valuation predicates. An extension is wim-constructible if generated by a well-ordered sequence (x_i), with v(x_i−E⟨x_j:j&lt;i⟩) having no maximum at every stage; ⟨ ⟩ denotes T-definable closure.

**Source.** Pietro Freni. *T-convexity, Weakly Immediate Types, and T-λ-Spherical Completions of o-minimal Structures*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07646v6) Location: v6 Question 3.32(1), p. 25; Definitions 2.8,3.15.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2567, 2568, checked 7 October 2026: 14 August 2026 v6 explicitly retains both questions; later targeted search found no resolution; checked 7 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://etheses.whiterose.ac.uk/id/eprint/36168/) · [2](https://etheses.whiterose.ac.uk/id/eprint/36168/1/Thesis-revised.pdf) · [3](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.07646v6)

## Q2569. Minimal computable-topological degrees

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.6) · **Collection** 26

For every compact metrizable X with a computable presentation, does every minimal element of CT(X) contain a computably compact presentation?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2569, 2570, 2571: 2025 preprint introduction. Setup for questions 2569, 2570, 2571: A computable compact metric presentation has a numbered dense set with uniformly computable distances. It is computably compact if its finite basic-open covers are computably enumerable. Put M≤ctN when a computable bijection N→M exists. CT(X) is the quotient of all computable presentations homeomorphic to X by mutual ≤ct.

**Source.** Noam Greenberg; Alexander Melnikov; Isabella Scott; Daniel Turetsky. *Comparing Presentations of Compact Spaces*. [primary source](https://homepages.ecs.vuw.ac.nz/~melnikal/2025_top_red.pdf) Location: Question 1.6, p. 5 (older author copy: Question 1.2, p. 3).

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2569, 2570, 2571, checked 7 October 2026: Current author PDF retains these; inspected September 2026 continuation and found no resolution of these exact targets; checked 7 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://heertern.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/a-reduction-on-compact-polish-spaces.pdf) · [2](https://homepages.ecs.vuw.ac.nz/~greenberg/Papers/92-top_red.pdf)

## Q2572. Effective local connectedness up to homeomorphism

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 26

Does every computably compact, locally connected space have a homeomorphic computable presentation that is effectively locally connected?

**Context.** Origin for question 2572: §1.3.2, before Theorem 1.5. Setup for question 2572: A computable metric presentation has a dense sequence with computable distances. It is computably compact when its finite basic-open covers are computably enumerable. Effective local connectedness means computing, from a name of x and r>0, a connected open C with x∈C⊆B(x,r), represented as a union of basic open balls.

**Source.** Kai Jun Khoo; Heer Tern Koh; Alexander Melnikov; Keng Meng Ng; Benjamin Shirley. *Effective Categoricity of Compacta*. [primary source](https://heertern.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/effective-categoricity-of-compacta.pdf) Location: §1.3.2, p.4; Definition 4.1, p.9.

<a id="q2573"></a>

## Q2573. First-order logic of IZF

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 26

Give an explicit characterization of Taut(IZF).

**Context.** Origin for questions 2573, 2574: IZF question follows Friedman–Ščedrov; extensibility question is joint work with Rosalie Iemhoff. Setup for question 2573: Taut(IZF) consists of equality-free first-order formulas whose every interpretation in intuitionistic Zermelo–Fraenkel set theory with Collection is provable there. Predicates receive arity-matching set-theoretic formulas; functions receive IZF-provably total single-valued definable graphs; constants receive IZF-provably unique definable objects. Substitution commutes with logical operations and does not relativize quantifiers.

**Source.** Robert Paßmann. *Logical Structure of Constructive Set Theories*. 2023. Advisor(s): Benedikt Löwe, Rosalie Iemhoff. [primary source](https://eprints.illc.uva.nl/id/document/12675) Location: §2.2, after Theorem 2.2.15, printed p. 24; Definitions 2.1.7–2.1.8 and 2.2.2, pp. 16–20.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2573, 2574, checked 7 October 2026: Explicit thesis questions; current author list and targeted searches located no matching resolution; checked 7 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.robertpassmann.eu/research) · [2](https://doi.org/10.1098/rsta.2022.0018) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.03105)


<a id="q2574"></a>

## Q2574. Choice extensibility over CZF

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.1.11) · **Collection** 26

Is the Axiom of Choice CZF-extensible?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2573, 2574: IZF question follows Friedman–Ščedrov; extensibility question is joint work with Rosalie Iemhoff. Setup for question 2574: CZF is constructive Zermelo–Fraenkel set theory. AC is CZF-extensible if every Kripke model M⊨CZF+AC on a frame K extends, unchanged on K, to a model on K with a new root below all old nodes that forces AC.

**Source.** Robert Paßmann. *Logical Structure of Constructive Set Theories*. 2023. Advisor(s): Benedikt Löwe, Rosalie Iemhoff. [primary source](https://eprints.illc.uva.nl/id/document/12675) Location: Question 6.1.11, printed p. 69; Definition 6.1.1, p. 65.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2573, 2574, checked 7 October 2026: Explicit thesis questions; current author list and targeted searches located no matching resolution; checked 7 October 2026.

<a id="q2575"></a>

## Q2575. Failure of coalgebraic Janin–Walukiewicz

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 26

Does some set functor T admit a behaviourally invariant MSO_T formula inequivalent, over pointed T-coalgebras, to every μML_T formula?

**Context.** Origin for question 2575: Enqvist–Seifan–Venema 2017; §4.7(4) credits an anonymous referee with the counterexample question. Setup for question 2575: Let T:Set→Set. A pointed T-model is (S,σ,V,s), with σ:S→TS and V assigning state subsets to variables. μML_T uses all finite-arity monotone predicate liftings and positive μ/ν binders. MSO_T allows Boolean operations, quantification over state subsets, inclusion p⊆q, sr(p) meaning V(p)={s}, and λ(p,q₁,…,qₙ) meaning σ(t)∈λ\_S(V(q₁),…,V(qₙ)) for every t∈V(p). Behavioural equivalence identifies states mapped together by valuation-preserving coalgebra morphisms into one model.

**Source.** Fatemeh Seifan. *Coalgebraic Fixpoint Logic: Expressivity and Completeness Results*. 2024. Advisor(s): Yde Venema, Helle Hvid Hansen, Sebastian Enqvist. [primary source](https://eprints.illc.uva.nl/id/document/12920) Location: §4.7(4), printed p. 138; Definitions 2.4.12 and4.2.3.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2575, checked 7 October 2026: Reaffirmed in 2024 thesis; targeted later searches found no counterexample or general theorem; checked 7 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://lmcs.episciences.org/3756/pdf)


<a id="q2576"></a>

## Q2576. Finitizing cyclic linear-logic proofs

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 26

Is every sequent provable in μMALL^ω also provable in finitary μMALL?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2576, 2577: Earlier proof-theoretic problems reaffirmed in the dissertation. Setup for question 2576: μMALL is propositional multiplicative-additive linear logic with least/greatest fixed points. μMALL^ω consists of regular unfolding proofs satisfying the standard infinite-branch trace condition; finitary μMALL uses the invariant coinduction rule. Cuts are permitted in both.

**Source.** Johannes Kloibhofer. *Cycles with Annotations: Non-Wellfounded Proof Theory of Modal Fixpoint Logics*. 2026. Advisor(s): Yde Venema, Bahareh Afshari. [primary source](https://eprints.illc.uva.nl/id/document/13116) Location: Remark 4.3.17, printed pp. 95–96; CSL 2018 article 35, Definitions 1–9.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2576, 2577, checked 7 October 2026: Both remain open in the 2026 thesis; the earlier game-logic completeness proof fails through Clo. No later resolution located; checked 7 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://jkloibhofer.github.io/research/) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.06846) · [3](https://drops.dagstuhl.de/doi/10.4230/LIPIcs.CSL.2018.35) · [4](https://logic.kastel.kit.edu/ap/pub/GLs.pdf)


<a id="q2577"></a>

## Q2577. Completeness of original game logic

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 26

Is Parikh’s original Hilbert calculus complete for propositional game logic?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2576, 2577: Earlier proof-theoretic problems reaffirmed in the dissertation. Setup for question 2577: Atomic games act by monotone maps P(S)→P(S). Game terms use composition, angelic choice, tests, finite iteration and dual; formulas use Boolean connectives and game modalities. Completeness means every formula valid in all such game models is derivable.

**Source.** Johannes Kloibhofer. *Cycles with Annotations: Non-Wellfounded Proof Theory of Modal Fixpoint Logics*. 2026. Advisor(s): Yde Venema, Bahareh Afshari. [primary source](https://eprints.illc.uva.nl/id/document/13116) Location: §4.4, item 3, printed p. 97.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2576, 2577, checked 7 October 2026: Both remain open in the 2026 thesis; the earlier game-logic completeness proof fails through Clo. No later resolution located; checked 7 October 2026.

## Q2578. Non-first-order basis spectrum

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.5.2) · **Collection** 26

Is there a κ-computable free abelian group G for which a κ-degree d computes a basis exactly when d≰\_κ∅^(n) for every n<ω?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2578, 2579: Linus Richter. On the Definability and Complexity of Sets and Structures. PhD, Victoria University of Wellington, 2024. Advisors: Dan Turetsky. Origin for questions 2578, 2579: Thesis Question 4.5.2, printed p. 86; primary paper §5. Setup for questions 2578, 2579: Assume V=L and let κ be a regular uncountable cardinal. Computability and degrees are admissible κ-computability over L_κ. Groups have universe κ and κ-computable operations; a basis freely generates the abelian group. ∅^(α) denotes the κ-computability jump hierarchy.

**Source.** Noam Greenberg; Linus Richter; Saharon Shelah; Dan Turetsky. *More on Bases of Uncountable Free Abelian Groups*. 2024. [primary source](https://homepages.ecs.vuw.ac.nz/~greenberg/Papers/71-groupsBases.pdf) Location: §5, printed p. 10; thesis Question 4.5.2.

## Q2580. Banach categoricity at every finite level

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2) · **Collection** 26

For every n≥1, is there a computable real Banach space that is Δ⁰\_(n+1)-categorical but not Δ⁰\_n-categorical?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2580, 2581: Question 2 credited to Melnikov; Question 8 follows Bosserhoff’s presentation-dependent obstruction. Setup for question 2580: A computable real Banach presentation has a dense sequence with computable norm and vector operations. Δ⁰\_n-categorical means any two computable presentations admit a surjective linear isometry computable in ∅^(n−1).

**Source.** Rod Downey; Noam Greenberg; Long Qian. *Some Open Questions and Recent Results on Computable Banach Spaces*. [primary source](https://homepages.ecs.vuw.ac.nz/~greenberg/Papers/86-banach_spaces_revue.pdf) Location: Question 2, printed p. 5.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2580, 2581, checked 7 October 2026: 2024 explicit questions; 2026 follow-on article and targeted searches yield no matching resolution; checked 7 October 2026.

<a id="q2581"></a>

## Q2581. An intrinsically noncomputable Schauder basis

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 8) · **Collection** 26

Does a computably presentable real Banach space X with a Schauder basis exist such that no computable presentation of X has a computable Schauder basis?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2580, 2581: Question 2 credited to Melnikov; Question 8 follows Bosserhoff’s presentation-dependent obstruction. Setup for question 2581: A computable real Banach space has a numbered dense sequence with uniformly computable distances, addition, scalar multiplication and norm; computation uses fast-Cauchy names. A Schauder basis is a sequence giving every vector a unique norm-convergent scalar expansion.

**Source.** Rod Downey; Noam Greenberg; Long Qian. *Some Open Questions and Recent Results on Computable Banach Spaces*. [primary source](https://homepages.ecs.vuw.ac.nz/~greenberg/Papers/86-banach_spaces_revue.pdf) Location: Question 8, printed p. 8.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2580, 2581, checked 7 October 2026: 2024 explicit questions; 2026 follow-on article and targeted searches yield no matching resolution; checked 7 October 2026.

## Q2582. Two-move memory for Nonempty

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 26

Whenever II has a winning strategy in this game, must II have a winning 2-tactic?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2582: see question 2533. Origin for question 2582: Galvin–Telgársky memory question. Setup for question 2582: On a nonempty topological space X, players I and II alternately choose nonempty open U₀⊇V₀⊇U₁⊇V₁⊇⋯. II wins if their intersection is nonempty. A 2-tactic chooses V₀ from U₀ and V_n, n≥1, using only (U_(n−1),U_n), not the round or earlier history.

**Source.** Marcel Scherer. *Typical Spectra and non-commutative Choquet Theory*. 2025. Advisor(s): Michael Hartz. [primary source](https://jahrbib.sulb.uni-saarland.de/bitstream/20.500.11880/40573/1/Online_UdS_Marcel_Scherer.pdf) Location: After Example 2.1.6, printed pp. 13–14, PDF pp. 23–24.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2582, checked 7 October 2026: Thesis explicitly leaves this open; checked 7 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://docs.google.com/document/d/11gWpVi5gFqlZmepeiNsz-SOrMyJi6l6EjsaDAWL3H5o/edit?usp=sharing) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/1912.03327) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.05508) · [4](https://sites.google.com/view/marcel-scherer/start)

## Q2583. Atomless lattice inside punctual rationals

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 26

Does the countable atomless Boolean algebra embed in PR(Q) preserving every binary join and meet?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2583: Ellen Hammatt. Arriving on Time: Punctuality in Structures, Isomorphisms and 1-Decidability. PhD, Victoria University of Wellington, 2025. Advisors: Alexander (Sasha) Melnikov; Dan Turetsky. Origin for question 2583: Explicit question after Theorem 1.2; continues Hammatt’s doctoral punctual-degree work. Setup for question 2583: Punctual copies of (Q,<) have domain N and primitive-recursive order. A≤prB means a primitive-recursive order isomorphism A→B exists; inverses need not be primitive recursive. PR(Q) is the quotient by mutual reducibility.

**Source.** Marina Dorzhieva; Ellen Hammatt. *Lattice Embeddings and Punctual Linear Orders*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/jsl.2025.10106) Location: §1, paragraph after Theorem 1.2, pp. 3–4; Definitions 1.1–1.2.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.5.1) · **Collection** 27

For every computable distributive lattice L=L₀⊔L₁ with computable partition, do a computably categorical A and a lattice embedding h into the c.e. degrees exist such that A is categorical relative to every h(L₀)-degree and to no h(L₁)-degree?

Origin for questions 2666, 2667: Thesis Questions 1.5.1–1.5.2; distinct from the September 2026 fixed-degree results. Setup for questions 2666, 2667: A computable structure A is categorical relative to degree d if every d-computable copy has a d-computable isomorphism with A. Structures have domain N in a computable language.

## Q2668. Arithmetic fixed-point reduction

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 8.1) · **Collection** 27

Is FP(Σ⁰₂)≤ᵃ\_W ATR₂?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2668, 2669: Yudai Suzuki. Studies on Partial Impredicativity in Formal Systems of Arithmetic and Computability Theory. Doctor of Science, Tohoku University, 2024. Advisor: Keita Yokoyama. Origin for questions 2668, 2669: Thesis Question 3.2.11; reaffirmed in APAL 175(2024),103488, §8. Setup for questions 2668, 2669: FP(Σ⁰₂) takes a formula-code and real parameter defining an inclusion-monotone F:P(N)→P(N), and returns X=F(X). ATR₂ takes a countable linear order L and A⊆N; it returns a descending L-sequence or a hierarchy with X_i=A when i is least and X_i=(⊕\_{j<\_L i}X_j)′ otherwise. ≤ᵃ\_W uses arithmetical pre/post-processing; ≤\_W uses computable pre/post-processing.

**Source.** Yudai Suzuki; Keita Yokoyama. *Searching problems above arithmetical transfinite recursion*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.07321) Location: Question 8.1(1), p.33; Definitions 2.7–2.9,3.3 and Corollary 3.4.

## Q2670. Four-color pigeonhole versus ADS

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.3) · **Collection** 27

Can an algorithm, given any c:N→4, computably produce a countably infinite linear order such that any infinite ascending or descending sequence through it, together with c, computably yields an infinite c-monochromatic set?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2670, 2671, 2672: Giovanni Soldà. Calibrating the complexity of combinatorics: reverse mathematics and Weihrauch degrees of some principles related to Ramsey’s theorem. PhD, University of Leeds, 2021. Advisors: Paul Shafer; Michael Rathjen. Origin for questions 2670, 2671, 2672: Thesis Questions 2.2.12,2.4.2,2.4.16; primary paper Questions 5.3,7.5,7.16. Setup for questions 2670, 2671, 2672: For infinite simple graphs on N, given by their adjacency relation, wRS outputs infinite H where each v∈H has 0,1 or infinitely many H-neighbors; wRS⁰ disallows 1. RT²₂ takes c:[N]²→2 to an infinite homogeneous set. ≤\_W uses computable pre/post-processing and one oracle call; \* is the Weihrauch compositional product.

**Source.** Marta Fiori-Carones; Paul Shafer; Giovanni Soldà. *An inside/outside Ramsey theorem and recursion theory*. 2021. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2006.16969) Location: Question 5.3, p. 18 (arXiv v2).

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2670, 2671, 2672, checked 7 October 2026: Primary-source questions retained; later targeted searches found no matching solution.

<a id="q2673"></a>

## Q2673. Width-two Rival–Sands strength

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.9) · **Collection** 27

Over RCA₀, does SADS imply RS₂ for all P of width≤2?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2673, 2674: see question 2670. Origin for questions 2673, 2674: Width-two question: thesis Question 3.5.6 and paper Question 6.9. Cofinite-strength question: paper Question 6.7, following thesis §3.6. Setup for questions 2673, 2674: Posets are countably infinite; width≤k excludes (k+1)-element antichains. RS_k requests an infinite chain C such that every p is comparable with none or infinitely many C-members; (0,cof)-RS_k replaces “infinitely many” by “all but finitely many”. Subscript <∞ allows arbitrary finite width. SADS gives an infinite ascending/descending sequence in any infinite linear order whose elements each have finitely many predecessors or finitely many successors.

**Source.** Marta Fiori-Carones; Alberto Marcone; Paul Shafer; Giovanni Soldà. *(Extra)ordinary equivalences with the ascending/descending sequence principle*. 2021. [primary source](https://peshafer.github.io/FioriCaronesMarconeShaferSoldaEquivalencesADS.pdf) Location: Question 6.9, p. 26; thesis Question 3.5.6.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2673, 2674, checked 7 October 2026: The 2024 JSL article and author manuscript retain these questions; no matching later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://etheses.whiterose.ac.uk/id/eprint/29548/1/Solda_G_Mathematics_PhD_2021.pdf)

## Q2675. Fraïssé in ATR₀

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.5.6) · **Collection** 27

Does ATR₀ prove that every sequence (L_n) of countable linear orders contains i&lt;j with an embedding L_i→L_j?

<a id="q2676"></a>

## Q2676. Monotone Π⁰₁ density

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.2.5) · **Collection** 27

Is there a total recursive map d taking pairs of Π⁰₁ sentences to Π⁰₁ sentences, extensional and monotone in each coordinate for this order, such that whenever ψ strictly implies φ, ψ strictly implies d(φ,ψ) and d(φ,ψ) strictly implies φ?

**Context.** Origin for question 2676: Question 2.2.5, attributed to Shavrukov and Visser. Setup for question 2676: EA is elementary arithmetic with exponentiation. Order Π⁰₁ sentences modulo EA-provable equivalence by provable implication; “strict” means implication without its converse.

**Source.** James Walsh. *Reflection Principles and Ordinal Analysis*. University of California, Berkeley, 2020. Advisor(s): Paolo Mancosu; Antonio Montalbán. [primary source](https://escholarship.org/content/qt9x943724/qt9x943724.pdf) Location: Question 2.2.5, p. 25.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2676, checked 7 October 2026: Explicit thesis question; later work and 2026 survey checked without locating a matching solution.


<a id="q2677"></a>

## Q2677. True-point coincidence with consistency

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 8.18) · **Collection** 27

If every g(φ) is Π⁰₁ and T+φ+Con_T^α(φ)⊢g(φ) for every sentence φ, must some true φ and β≤α satisfy T⊢(φ∧g(φ))↔(φ∧Con_T^β(φ))?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2677: see question 2676. Origin for question 2677: Thesis Question 2.5.8; reaffirmed as Question 8.18 in BSL 32(2026),47–81. Setup for question 2677: Fix a sound r.e. same-language T⊇EA. Let (D,≺) be an elementary presentation well-ordered in N; EA proves linearity, successor closure, and correctness of elementary predicates for zero, limits and successor. Fix α∈D. Set Con_T^0(φ)=⊤ and Con_T^α(φ)↔∀β≺α Con(T+φ+Con_T^β(φ)). Let g be a total recursive monotone operator on sentences, meaning that for all sentences φ,ψ, T⊢φ→ψ implies T⊢g(φ)→g(ψ).

**Source.** James Walsh. *On the hierarchy of natural theories*. 2020. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.05794) Location: Question 8.18, pp. 24–25; suitable notations: Evitable iterates (2023), §2.2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2677, checked 7 October 2026: 2026 publication retains this true-point question; stronger cone version is already refuted and excluded.

**Further links.** [1](https://escholarship.org/content/qt9x943724/qt9x943724.pdf) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.01174)

## Q2682. Baire co-totality and discontinuity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.3) · **Collection** 27

Must every Baire co-total f satisfy DIS×f≤\_W f?

**Context.** Origin for question 2682: Open Question 6.3 in Computability 14(2025),73–94. Setup for question 2682: Let f:⊆N^N⇉N^N. For g:⊆N^N⇉N^N, Tg agrees with g on its domain and returns all N^N elsewhere. f is Baire co-total if f≤\_W Tg implies f≤\_W g for every such g. For a fixed universal partial computable U:N^N→N^N, DIS(p)={q:q≠U(p)}, with all q allowed if U(p) diverges. × is parallel product.

**Source.** Uri Andrews; Steffen Lempp; Alberto Marcone; Joseph S. Miller; Manlio Valenti. *A jump operator on the Weihrauch degrees*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13163) Location: Open Question 6.3, p. 24; Definition 3.20.

**Further links.** [1](https://people.math.wisc.edu/~slempp/papers/jump.pdf)

## Q2768. Higher dependence of pure fields

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.3.6) · **Collection** 28

For every n≥2, must every NIPₙ field in the pure language of rings be NIP?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2768: Blaise Marius Rémy Boissonneau. PhD, University of Münster, 2022. Advisors: Franziska Jahnke. Origin for question 2768: Earlier conjecture repeated in the dissertation, not claimed as student-origin. Setup for question 2768: A formula φ(x;y₁,…,yₙ) has IPₙ if, for every m, some arrays (aᵢʲ)ᵢ&lt;m and parameters b_S satisfy φ(b_S;aᵢ₁¹,…,aᵢₙⁿ) exactly when (i₁,…,iₙ)∈S, for every S⊆{0,…,m−1}ⁿ. A theory is NIPₙ if no formula has IPₙ; NIP means NIP₁.

**Source.** Blaise Boissonneau. *Combinatorial Complexity in Henselian Valued Fields: Pushing Anscombe–Jahnke up the Ladder*. University of Münster, 2022. Advisor(s): Franziska Jahnke. [primary source](https://noah.nrw/download/pdf/6824765.pdf) Location: Thesis Conjecture 2.3.6, p. 32; NIPₙ CHIPS Conjecture 2.4.

<a id="q2769"></a>

## Q2769. Defect of the canonical valuation in divisible-tame type

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.6.18) · **Collection** 28

Let K be non-henselian, t-henselian of divisible-tame type, of characteristic p>0, and not separably closed. If L≡K admits a nontrivial henselian valuation, must (L,v_L) be defectless?

**Context.** Origin for question 2769: Explicit thesis question, also Ketelsen–Ramello–Szewczyk, JSL Question 6.18. Setup for question 2769: A field is non-henselian if it admits no nontrivial henselian valuation. Divisible-tame type means that some elementarily equivalent field has a nontrivial henselian, defectless valuation with divisible value group and perfect residue field. A henselian valuation w is defectless when [E:F]=[wE:wF][Ew:Fw] for every finite E/F. The canonical henselian valuation v_F is the coarsest with separably closed residue, if one exists, and otherwise the finest henselian valuation.

**Source.** Simone Ramello. *Towards a Model Theory of Frobenius Lifts: Valued Difference Fields and Definable Henselian Valuations*. University of Münster, 2025. Advisor(s): Martin Hils; Franziska Jahnke. [primary source](https://d-nb.info/1372173587/34) Location: Thesis Question 3.6.18, p. 40; JSL Question 6.18.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2769, checked 7 October 2026: Current journal question retained; bounded later searches found no resolution through 7 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1017/jsl.2024.55) · [2](https://simoneramello.it/?page_id=408) · [3](https://www.uni-muenster.de/FB10/Service/show_article.shtml?brettid=&id=10487)

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 9.1.2) · **Collection** 28

## Q2773. ω-stable bounded theory with unbounded type

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 8.1) · **Collection** 28

Does a complete bounded ω-stable theory with an unbounded complete type exist?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2773, 2774: Current journal §8 explicitly asks these questions. Author is a Wisconsin–Madison PhD student; no completed dissertation or advisor attribution inferred. Setup for questions 2773, 2774: Languages are countable. A theory is bounded if axiomatizable by formulas with a fixed finite bound on alternating quantifier blocks, beginning universally. A complete type p(x̄) over ∅, in finitely many variables, is unbounded if the theory p(c̄), with new constants, has no such bound. ω-stability means countably many types over each countable parameter set.

**Source.** Hongyu Zhu. *A Complete Bounded Theory with Unbounded Types*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/jsl.2026.10208) Location: Current journal Question 8.1.

## Q2775. Existential definitions under tilting

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 28

If O_v is parameter-free existentially definable in K, must O_{v♭} be parameter-free existentially definable in K♭?

**Context.** Origin for question 2775: Explicit open direction in §5, reached through the Jahnke–Ramello doctoral genealogy. Setup for question 2775: Let (K,v) be a mixed-characteristic (0,p) perfectoid field: complete, nondiscretely rank-one valued, with Frobenius surjective on O_v/p. Write (K♭,v♭) for its tilt. Parameter-free existential definability means definability of the valuation ring by an existential formula in the pure ring language, without parameters.

**Source.** Gessica Alecci; Ihsane Hadeg; Franziska Jahnke; Margarete Ketelsen; Isabella Negrini. *Definability via the Tilting Correspondence*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23466) Location: §5 opening, p. 11; Corollary 5.3 and Example 5.4.

<a id="q2777"></a>

## Q2777. One-dimensional spectra of GP²

**Status:** Solved here: proved · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 28

For every GP² sentence φ, is {|M|: M is finite and M⊨φ} semilinear?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2777, 2778: Two explicit residual questions after Theorem 38, following the De Bortoli doctoral pass. Setup for questions 2777, 2778: Over finite structures, GP² starts from atoms U(x),U(y),R(x,y),R(y,x) in a finite unary/binary vocabulary and uses Boolean operations, unguarded quantification of unary formulas, and local tests Σᵢ aᵢ·#y[Rᵢ(x,y)∧φᵢ(x,y)] ⋈ b, also with reversed guards and swapped variables. Coefficients and thresholds are integers; ⋈ is = or an inequality. No modulus tests are included. Semilinear subsets of N are ultimately periodic.

**Source.** Michael Benedikt; Chia-Hsuan Lu; Tony Tan. *Analysis of Logics with Arithmetic*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.CSL.2026.27) Location: §6, paragraph after Theorem 38, p. 27:16.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2777, 2778, checked 7 October 2026: Explicitly open in the 2026 proceedings; no later resolution located through 7 October 2026.

**Results.**

- **Proved**: For every GP² sentence in the local positive binary-guard syntax, finite models split into finitely many closed-subformula truth profiles. Each profile is closed under disjoint union, so its positive size set is additive and is a finite union of arithmetic progressions. This proves ordinary one-dimensional semilinearity, without an effective construction or a multivariate/global-cardinality conclusion. Independently reviewed internally; finite checks support the proof. No novelty or external-review claim. [Details](../../solutions/b1-continuation-2026-10-12/gp2/Q2777-RESULT.md).
- **Proved** (B1-CONTINUATION-Q2777: Finite total-size spectra of GP² are semilinear): For every GP² sentence in the local positive binary-guard syntax, finite models split into finitely many closed-subformula truth profiles. Each profile is closed under disjoint union, so its positive size set is additive and is a finite union of arithmetic progressions. This proves ordinary one-dimensional semilinearity, without an effective construction or a multivariate/global-cardinality conclusion. Independently reviewed internally; finite checks support the proof. No novelty or external-review claim. Remaining/limits: No effective spectrum representation or novelty claim. [Report](../../solutions/b1-continuation-2026-10-12/gp2/Q2777-RESULT.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.03574)


<a id="q2778"></a>

## Q2778. Global cardinality constraints for GP²

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 28

Is finite satisfiability decidable for GP² sentences conjoined with global linear integer constraints among cardinalities of unary GP²-definable sets?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2777, 2778: Two explicit residual questions after Theorem 38, following the De Bortoli doctoral pass. Setup for questions 2777, 2778: Over finite structures, GP² starts from atoms U(x),U(y),R(x,y),R(y,x) in a finite unary/binary vocabulary and uses Boolean operations, unguarded quantification of unary formulas, and local tests Σᵢ aᵢ·#y[Rᵢ(x,y)∧φᵢ(x,y)] ⋈ b, also with reversed guards and swapped variables. Coefficients and thresholds are integers; ⋈ is = or an inequality. No modulus tests are included. Semilinear subsets of N are ultimately periodic.

**Source.** Michael Benedikt; Chia-Hsuan Lu; Tony Tan. *Analysis of Logics with Arithmetic*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.CSL.2026.27) Location: §6, paragraph after Theorem 38, p. 27:16.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2777, 2778, checked 7 October 2026: Explicitly open in the 2026 proceedings; no later resolution located through 7 October 2026.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: Finite satisfiability is decidable for GP² conjoined with homogeneous integer linear comparisons of unary definable cardinalities, and with one-sided L>=k for k>=0 (equivalently L<=k for k<=0). Relativized token ownership, injections, bijections and local surplus centers reduce these cases to ordinary GP². Fixed affine equalities and the full catalog question remain unresolved after five distinct failed routes. This is a restricted theorem, not decidability or undecidability of Q2778. [Details](../../solutions/b1-continuation-2026-10-12/gp2/Q2778-ATTEMPTS.md).
- **Partial result** (B1-CONTINUATION-Q2778: Decidable homogeneous globals; arbitrary affine globals unresolved): Finite satisfiability is decidable for GP² conjoined with homogeneous integer linear comparisons of unary definable cardinalities, and with one-sided L>=k for k>=0 (equivalently L<=k for k<=0). Relativized token ownership, injections, bijections and local surplus centers reduce these cases to ordinary GP². Fixed affine equalities and the full catalog question remain unresolved after five distinct failed routes. This is a restricted theorem, not decidability or undecidability of Q2778. Remaining/limits: The full target remains unresolved; five-failure limit reached. [Report](../../solutions/b1-continuation-2026-10-12/gp2/Q2778-ATTEMPTS.md).
- **Investigated, unresolved** (B1-CONTINUATION-STOP-REGISTER: Per-problem stopping record): Every registered target is locally solved, a retained audited solution, or stopped at its own five consecutive substantive full-target failures. Partial results do not reset counters; software, source and service errors are excluded. The original 30-question membership is preserved. Remaining/limits: A completed research run under the user's rule does not settle its unresolved mathematical questions. Same retained agent; private filing. [Report](../../solutions/b1-continuation-2026-10-12/STATUS.json).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.03574)


<a id="q2779"></a>

## Q2779. Higher-dependence composition lemma

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.27) · **Collection** 28

Let n≥2 and k,d≥1. Suppose the L-reduct of an L′-structure M is NIPₙ. For an L-formula φ(x₁,…,x_d), tuples y₀,…,y_{kn}, and arbitrary L′-definable functions f_t of k selected y-tuples (repetitions allowed) into M^{x_t}, must ψ(y₀;y₁,…,y_{kn})=φ(f₁(…),…,f_d(…)) be NIP_{kn}?

**Context.** Origin for question 2779: Explicit Conjecture 3.27 in the current revision, reached through the Boissonneau field-conjecture follow-up. Setup for question 2779: A formula φ(x;y₁,…,yₙ) has IPₙ if, for every m, some arrays (aᵢʲ)ᵢ&lt;m and parameters b_S satisfy φ(b_S;aᵢ₁¹,…,aᵢₙⁿ) exactly when (i₁,…,iₙ)∈S, for every S⊆{0,…,m−1}ⁿ. A theory is NIPₙ if no formula has IPₙ; NIP means NIP₁.

**Source.** Artem Chernikov; Nadja Hempel. *On n-Dependent Groups and Fields III: Multilinear Forms and Invariant Connected Components*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.19921v3) Location: Conjecture 3.27.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2779, checked 7 October 2026: Explicit conjecture on 18 September 2026; checked 7 October 2026.


<a id="q2877"></a>

## Q2877. Clopen finite-index layers

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.14) · **Collection** 29

Must every Dₙ, n≥1, be clopen in X?

**Context.** Origin for question 2877: Joint-paper questions, linked to the doctoral research. Setup for question 2877: A descriptive MS4-frame (X,R,E) has Stone space X, continuous preorder R and continuous equivalence E, satisfying xEyRy′⇒∃x′(xRx′Ey′). Continuous means closed successor sets and clopen inverse images of clopens. Its clopen Boolean algebra with R^{-1},E^{-1} is finitely generated. Set D₁=qmax(X), Dₙ₊₁=qmax(X∖⋃ᵢ≤ₙDᵢ), where qmax(Y)={x∈Y:∀y∈Y(xRy⇒yRx)}.

**Source.** Guram Bezhanishvili; Chase Meadors. *Local finiteness in varieties of MS4-algebras*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.16754v3) Location: Question 5.14, JSL p. 1251; preprint p. 19.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2877, checked 7 October 2026: September 2026 journal text checked; bounded later searches found no resolution.

<a id="q2880"></a>

## Q2880. Compact frame points and choice

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 29

Does “every nontrivial compact frame has a point” imply the axiom of choice?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2880: Ranjitha Raviprakash. McKinsey-Tarski algebras: an alternative pointfree approach to topology. PhD, New Mexico State University, 2025. Advisors: Guram Bezhanishvili. Origin for question 2880: Johnstone’s 1982 question explicitly repeated. Setup for question 2880: Work over ZF. A frame is a complete lattice in which finite meets distribute over arbitrary joins. It is compact when every family joining to 1 has a finite subfamily joining to 1. A point is a frame homomorphism to {0,1}.

**Source.** Guram Bezhanishvili; Sebastian D. Melzer; Ranjitha Raviprakash; Anna Laura Suarez. *Local compactness does not always imply spatiality*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.01645v2) Location: Remark 4.17(3), p. 11.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2880, checked 7 October 2026: Reaffirmed 25 September 2026; later bounded searches found no resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://commencement.nmsu.edu/Spring-25_Commencement_Program_FINAL.pdf)


<a id="q2974"></a>

## Q2974. Eventual strictness of pure maps in ZFC

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 26) · **Collection** 30

Does ZFC prove that every λ-accessible category admits a regular μ▷λ for which every μ-pure map is a strict monomorphism?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2974: Kristóf Kanalas. Topos-valued infinitary first-order categorical logic. PhD, Masaryk University, 2025. Advisors: Jiří Rosický. Origin for question 2974: Explicit primary-paper question removing a large-cardinal assumption. Setup for question 2974: A λ-accessible category has λ-filtered colimits and is generated under them by a set of λ-presentable objects. An object A is λ-presentable if Hom(A,−) preserves these colimits. A map f:X→Y is μ-pure if every square A→X, A→B, B→Y with μ-presentable A,B admits B→X making the upper triangle commute. A strict monomorphism is a joint equalizer of a set of parallel pairs. For regular infinite λ<μ, μ▷λ means every [ν]^{<λ}, ν<μ, has a cofinal subset of size<μ.

**Source.** Kristóf Kanalas. *Pure maps are strict monomorphisms*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.13448v3) Location: Question 26, p. 8; Theorem 24.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2974, checked 7 October 2026: Current source checked; bounded later searches found no resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://theses.cz/id/az3qxy/)

## Q2978. Two quantifier blocks with two power predicates

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 30

For every such a,b, is the ∃\*∀\* fragment of Th(Z;0,1,<,+,P_a,P_b) decidable?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2978, 2979: Toghrul Karimov. Algorithmic Verification of Linear Dynamical Systems. PhD, Saarland University, 2024. Advisors: Joël Ouaknine. Origin for questions 2978, 2979: Explicit residual fragments in the primary paper after its existential-predicate decidability theorem. Setup for questions 2978, 2979: Fix multiplicatively independent integers a,b>1: a^s=b^t for s,t∈N implies s=t=0. P_a={a^n:n≥0} and P_b={b^n:n≥0} are unary predicates. Theory decidability takes sentences as input; a,b are fixed.

**Source.** Toghrul Karimov; Florian Luca; Joris Nieuwveld; Joël Ouaknine; James Worrell. *On the Decidability of Presburger Arithmetic Expanded with Powers*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.05191v2) Location: §3, p. 7, final paragraph.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2978, 2979, checked 7 October 2026: Current source checked; bounded later searches found no resolution.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: For every fixed source-admissible pair of power bases, truth in the existential-universal predicate fragment is uniformly computably enumerable: enumerate outer integer tuples and decide the existential complement of each instantiated universal block with the two-base existential decision theorem. No effective outer-witness bound or terminating negative test is supplied. Full decidability remains unresolved. [Details](../../solutions/b1-continuation-2026-10-12/arithmetic/ATTEMPTS.md).
- **Partial result** (B1-CONTINUATION-Q2978: Two-block two-power truth is computably enumerable): For every fixed source-admissible pair of power bases, truth in the existential-universal predicate fragment is uniformly computably enumerable: enumerate outer integer tuples and decide the existential complement of each instantiated universal block with the two-base existential decision theorem. No effective outer-witness bound or terminating negative test is supplied. Full decidability remains unresolved. Remaining/limits: The full target remains unresolved; five-failure limit reached. ## Q2980. Repeated-root recurrence predicate

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 30

Is Th(N;0,1,<,+,U) decidable?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2980, 2981, 2982: see question 2978. Origin for questions 2980, 2981, 2982: Explicit primary-paper frontier examples and conjecture; prime problem is historical and repeated. Setup for questions 2980, 2981, 2982: All theories are first-order, with natural-number universe and the displayed symbols only. U={n·2^n:n≥0}, P is the prime predicate, and Q_r consists of positive integers divisible by no rth power of a prime.

**Source.** Toghrul Karimov; Joris Nieuwveld; Joël Ouaknine. *Rich Sequences and Decidability of Arithmetic Theories*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20415v1) Location: §1.4, p. 9.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2980, 2981, 2982, checked 7 October 2026: Explicitly open 17 September 2026; no later resolving claim found.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: Intersecting U={n 2^n} with powers of two gives exactly {2^(2^k+k):k>=0}. Its binary zero-run lengths have unbounded gaps and are not ultimately periodic, contradicting unary regularity. This obstructs a synchronous binary-automata method; it does not settle the first-order theory with U. [Details](../../solutions/b1-continuation-2026-10-12/arithmetic/ATTEMPTS.md).
- **Partial result** (B1-CONTINUATION-Q2980: The predicate {n 2^n} is not binary automatic): Intersecting U={n 2^n} with powers of two gives exactly {2^(2^k+k):k>=0}. Its binary zero-run lengths have unbounded gaps and are not ultimately periodic, contradicting unary regularity. This obstructs a synchronous binary-automata method; it does not settle the first-order theory with U. Remaining/limits: The full target remains unresolved; five-failure limit reached. ## Q3074. Strong indivisibility in REC

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 31

For every computable G not isomorphic to the infinite complete graph, its edgeless complement, or R, is there a computable partition V(G)=X₀⊔X₁ such that neither induced graph G[X_i] is computably isomorphic to G?

**Context.** Origin for question 3074: Explicit thesis question, following joint work with Dzhafarov and Solomon. Setup for question 3074: Graphs are countably infinite, simple and undirected, with computable vertex and edge relations. R is the countable random graph.

<a id="q3078"></a>

## Q3078. Differential largeness versus genericity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 31

Must every differentially large δ be generic: for every integer r≥0 and every X⊆K^{r+1} definable in the ring language with parameters from K, if its first-r-coordinate projection has dimension r, does some a∈K satisfy (a,δa,…,δ^r a)∈X?

**Context.** Origin for question 3078: Paper question; reached through Widawski’s dissertation genealogy. Setup for question 3078: Let K have characteristic zero and only the ring language. It is large if K is existentially closed in K((t)). A derivation δ is differentially large if K is large and every differential extension (L,δ) with K existentially closed in L also makes (K,δ) existentially closed in (L,δ). Dimension means dimension of Zariski closure.

**Source.** Elliot Kaplan; Christoph Kesting. *Generic derivations, differential largeness, and NTP₂*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13919) Location: Unnumbered Question, p. 3; Definitions 1.1–1.2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3078, checked 8 October 2026: Final APAL text and August 2026 rank paper checked; general converse remains explicitly open.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.apal.2026.103730)


<a id="q3081"></a>

## Q3081. Higher hierarchy inside NTP₂

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.6) · **Collection** 31

For every integer n≥3, must every NTP₂ and NSOP_{n+1} theory be NSOP_n?

**Context.** Origin for question 3081: Restates the higher hierarchy problem; not the separate NSOP₂=NSOP₃ problem. Setup for question 3081: SOP_n means that some definable relation has an infinite forward chain but no directed n-cycle; NSOP_n is its negation. NTP₂ excludes a formula φ(x;y) and array (b_ij) whose rows {φ(x;b_ij):j∈N} are 2-inconsistent while every transversal {φ(x;b_i,f(i)):i∈N} is consistent. Theories are complete first-order theories.

**Source.** Scott Mutchnik. *Some applications of the real strict order property hierarchy*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28740) Location: Question 1.6, p. 5; Definitions 1.1 and 2.3, pp. 3,10.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3081, checked 8 October 2026: The August NSOP₂=NSOP₃ proof claim does not state this higher-hierarchy conclusion.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.14) · **Collection** 32

Advisors: Jacques Duparc; Luca Motto Ros. Origin for question 3169: Paper question reached through Pitton’s completed doctoral genealogy. Setup for question 3169: Let κ be regular uncountable and I a proper κ-complete ideal containing all bounded subsets. On {}^κν, ν∈{2,κ}, use basic opens {x:f⊆x}, with dom(f)∈I. I is tall if every unbounded A⊆κ contains an unbounded member of I. The I-Borel hierarchy starts with these open sets and iterates complements and unions of at most κ sets.

## Q3173. Separating the last two Scott highness levels

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 32

Does some degree d have highness at ω₁^CK but fail highness for isomorphism?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3173, 3174: Two explicit concluding paper questions. Setup for questions 3173, 3174: SR(A) is the least α for which every finite-tuple automorphism orbit is definable by a parameter-free infinitary Σ\_α formula. A degree d is high at α if it computes an isomorphism between every two isomorphic computable structures of rank α. High for isomorphism removes the rank restriction. ω₁^CK is the least noncomputable ordinal.

**Source.** Johanna N. Y. Franklin; Dino Rossegger; Dan Turetsky. *Structural vs. computational complexity*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15196) Location: Question 1,p. 15; Definitions 1–2,pp. 1–2.

## Q3175. Removing weakening with polynomial proof cost

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 32

Does a polynomial p exist such that every CFLew proof π of an FLe-provable sequent has a CFLe proof of the same sequent of size at most p(|π|)?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3175, 3176: Explicit unresolved entries of Table1, discussed in §6. Setup for questions 3175, 3176: CFLe is classical full Lambek sequent calculus with exchange and cut, over {1,0,∧,∨,\*,→}, exactly Table3. Add both-side weakening to obtain CFLew, or both-side contraction to obtain CFLec. FLe is the single-conclusion counterpart of CFLe. Proofs are DAG-like; size counts all symbols. Only FLe-provable end-sequents are compared.

<a id="q3178"></a>

## Q3178. Uniform isomorphism highness at a minimal double jump

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 32

Does there exist a uniformly high-for-isomorphism degree d with d″≡\_T O?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3178, 3179: Explicit final-article open questions; double-jump problem repeated from earlier structural-highness work. Setup for questions 3178, 3179: Uniform highness means an oracle computes isomorphisms uniformly from indices for isomorphic computable structures; outputs may be partial otherwise. Loquacious highness on K uses one oracle procedure producing total functions for every pair of computable K-members, isomorphisms for isomorphic pairs, with Θ(i,j)=Θ(j,i)⁻¹ there. Σ₁¹-completeness uses computable many-one reductions producing pairs from K. O is Kleene’s complete Π₁¹ set.

**Source.** Wesley Calvert; Johanna N. Y. Franklin; Dan Turetsky. *Failure modes for structural highness notions*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1093/logcom/exaf051) Location: §3,paragraph after Theorem 3.10 (final HTML lines349ff).

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3178, 3179, checked 8 October 2026: Final Oxford HTML and current author material checked; no later resolution located.

## Q3180. First HOD disagreement at uncountable cofinality

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.1) · **Collection** 32

Is it consistent that HOD is cardinal-correct and Δ(HOD,V)=κ for a singular cardinal κ of uncountable cofinality?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3180, 3181: Explicit paper questions reached through Thei’s doctoral bibliography and later work. Setup for questions 3180, 3181: Work in ZFC. HOD is the hereditarily ordinal-definable inner model. Δ(HOD,V) is the least ordinal κ with P(κ)⊈HOD. “Cardinal-correct” means HOD and V have the same cardinals. Superscript HOD specifies internally computed cardinal ordinals; comparisons and stationarity are in V.

## Q3182. Double-successor compactness inside HOD

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 32

If κ is a strong limit singular cardinal of uncountable cofinality and {δ<κ:(δ^{++})^HOD≥δ⁺} is stationary, must (κ^{++})^HOD≥κ⁺?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3182, 3183: Explicit final-journal question, reached through Thei’s genealogy. Setup for questions 3182, 3183: Work in ZFC. Successor cardinals marked HOD are computed in the hereditarily ordinal-definable inner model; unmarked ones in V. Stationarity at κ means meeting every closed unbounded subset of κ. A regular uncountable λ is ω-strongly measurable in HOD if some nonzero η<λ satisfies (2^η)^HOD<λ and no HOD partition of {α<λ:cf(α)=ω} into η sets has every cell stationary in V.

**Source.** Gabriel Goldberg; Alejandro Poveda. *Compactness phenomena in HOD*. 2025. [primary source](https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/forum-of-mathematics-sigma/article/compactness-phenomena-in-hod/FFD6AD92EFDDDAC2D53A98CDE7A396AD) Location: §4,Question 1.

## Q3268. Distal expansions of Mekler groups

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.29) · **Collection** 33

If C admits a distal expansion, must M(C) admit one?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3268: Aristomenis-Dionysios (Aris) Papadopoulos. Model-Theoretic Considerations in Finite Combinatorics. PhD, 2024, University of Leeds. Advisors: Pantelis Eleftheriou; Dugald Macpherson. Origin for question 3268: Question in the final joint paper, reached through Papadopoulos’s doctoral genealogy. Setup for question 3268: Let C be a simple graph with at least two vertices and no triangles or 4-cycles such that, for every distinct v,w, some u∉{v,w} is adjacent to v but not w. Fix an odd prime p. M(C) is the free class-2 nilpotent group of exponent p on V(C), modulo commutation of generators joined by an edge. A distal expansion adds structure on the same domain and has distal complete theory.

**Source.** Blaise Boissonneau; Aris Papadopoulos; Pierre Touchard. *Mekler’s Construction and Murphy’s Law for 2-Nilpotent Groups*. 2026. [primary source](https://msp.org/pjm/2026/341-2/pjm-v341-n2-p02-p.pdf) Location: Question 4.29, p. 266.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3268, checked 8 October 2026: Final article retains the question; no later resolution located.

<a id="q3269"></a>

## Q3269. Ramsey structures beyond their dependence arity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3) · **Collection** 33

For each integer n≥2, is there such an ultrahomogeneous Ramsey structure that is NIP_n but is not n-ary?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3269: see question 3268. Origin for question 3269: Thesis question repeating joint work with Meir and Touchard. Setup for question 3269: A countable relational structure is ultrahomogeneous if every isomorphism between finite substructures extends to an automorphism. Its age is Ramsey if, for finite A,B in the age and r≥2, some C in the age makes every r-colouring of embeddings A→C constant on all embeddings into a copy of B. NIP_n excludes formulas independently encoding all subsets of a product of n infinite parameter sequences. An n-ary structure admits quantifier elimination in a relational language of arity at most n.

**Source.** Aris Papadopoulos. *Model-Theoretic Considerations in Finite Combinatorics*. University of Leeds, 2024. Advisor(s): Pantelis Eleftheriou and Dugald Macpherson. [primary source](https://etheses.whiterose.ac.uk/id/eprint/35815/1/mtcifc.pdf) Location: §4.7, Question 3, p. 159; joint-paper Question 6.3, p. 42.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3269, checked 8 October 2026: Current joint-paper version retains it; no later resolution located.

## Q3270. Sharp type-language Scott sentences

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.1) · **Collection** 33

Can T have at most countably many countable models while some countable model has no Π\_4^in(L_type(T)) Scott sentence?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3270, 3271: Hongyu Zhu. Current doctoral student, University of Wisconsin–Madison. Origin for questions 3270, 3271: Two explicit sharpness questions in the new primary paper. Setup for questions 3270, 3271: T is a complete ω-stable theory in a countable language L. L_type(T) adds a predicate for each complete finite-tuple type over ∅, interpreted by its realizations. Π\_k^in denotes the usual infinitary Π\_k hierarchy in L_{ω₁ω} over this expanded language. A Scott sentence characterizes one countable structure up to isomorphism; Π\_k^in-equivalence means agreement on all such sentences. Model counts are up to isomorphism.

**Source.** Hongyu Zhu. *The Type ω-Vaught’s Conjecture for ω-stable Theories*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09314v1) Location: Question 4.1, first bullet, p. 14; Definition 2.1.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3270, 3271, checked 8 October 2026: Latest v1 retains both; no later resolution located.

<a id="q3272"></a>

## Q3272. Lower-complexity recovery at infinite Scott ranks

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.5) · **Collection** 33

For which countable α≥ω+1 is there β<α and such an operator taking every copy of T_A to a copy of A, uniformly for A∈Mod(L) with SR(A)≤α?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3272: David Gonzalez. Countable Structures, Isomorphism, and Complexity. PhD, 2025, University of California, Berkeley. Advisors: Antonio Montalbán. Origin for question 3272: Explicit question in the doctoral-branch follow-on paper. Setup for question 3272: For a countable structure A in a finite relational language L, T_A has root the empty tuple. A node representing distinct a₁,…,a_n has infinitely many children for each a∈A\\{a₁,…,a_n}, representing its extension by a. Labels record complete atomic types; the language contains the root-fixing predecessor function and label predicates. SR(A) is the least γ admitting a Π\_{γ+1} infinitary Scott sentence. Δ\_β⁰ operators are Δ\_β⁰-measurable maps between spaces of structures, not necessarily effective.

**Source.** David Gonzalez; Matthew Harrison-Trainor; Julia Knight. *Recovering a structure from its tree of tuples*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05611v1) Location: Question 4.5, p. 23; Definition 2.12, p. 6; footnote2, p. 2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3272, checked 8 October 2026: Latest v1 retains it and accounts for the cited tree-of-tuples erratum.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.davidgonzalezlogic.com/) · [2](https://math.berkeley.edu/publications/countable-structures-isomorphism-and-complexity)


<a id="q3273"></a>

## Q3273. Analytic Van Douwen families for ideals

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.1) · **Collection** 33

Which coanalytic J admit an analytic Van Douwen J-MED family?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3273, 3274, 3275: Paper questions; Luo’s work predates doctoral enrollment. Setup for questions 3273, 3274, 3275: J⊇Fin is a proper ideal on ω; Eq(f,g)={n:f(n)=g(n)}. A J-MED family E⊆ω^ω has pairwise J-small agreement sets, and every h agrees with some f∈E on a J-positive set. Van Douwen means maximality after restriction to every J-positive X. Finite productivity requires every finite tuple h_i to have f_i∈E with ⋂Eq(h_i,f_i)∉J. The ω-centered version requires every sequence h_i to have f_i∈E and B∉J with B\Eq(h_i,f_i)∈J for all i. A P-point U is a nonprincipal ultrafilter whose countable subfamilies have pseudointersections in U; f≡\_U g iff Eq(f,g)∈U.

**Source.** Jialiang He; Jintao Luo; Hang Zhang. *Van Douwen Families, Productivity and Ultrafilter Maximality*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02350v1) Location: Question 6.1, p. 33; Definitions 2.1 and3.1.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3273, 3274, 3275, checked 8 October 2026: October v1 retains all three.

<a id="q3279"></a>

## Q3279. Effective amenable actions of exact groups

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.8) · **Collection** 33

Which countable exact groups admit such a computable presentation and computable amenable action on such a computably compact space?

**Context.** Origin for question 3279: Explicit primary-paper question. Setup for question 3279: A countable group presentation lists generators and decides whether each word equals 1. A nonempty compact metric space has a dense named sequence with uniformly computable distances and computable finite 2^{-k}-nets. An action is computable here if images of named points under named generators can be approximated uniformly. Amenability means continuous maps m_i:X→Prob(G) satisfy sup_x||g·m_i(x)−m_i(gx)||₁→0 for every g∈G.

**Source.** Isaac Goldbring. *Computable presentations of reduced group C\*-algebras of hyperbolic groups*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05986v1) Location: Question 2.8, p. 5; Definitions 2.1 and2.4, pp. 3–4.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3279, checked 8 October 2026: Both October papers checked; no classification of effective amenable actions is given.

## Q3280. Subset closure of extensional well-quasi-orders

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.15) · **Collection** 33

Over RCA₀, determine the strength of “every restriction of an extensional-wqo is extensional-wqo”: is it provable in RCA₀, or equivalent to WKL₀?

**Context.** Origin for question 3280: Marcone’s explicit question, reidentified through Buriola’s doctoral branch. Setup for question 3280: A countable quasi-order is extensional-wqo if every linear extension of its quotient by x∼y iff x≤y and y≤x is a well-order. The restriction to R⊆Q uses the inherited quasi-order. RCA₀ is the usual recursive-comprehension base theory; WKL₀ additionally asserts that every infinite binary tree has an infinite path.

**Source.** Alberto Marcone. *The reverse mathematics of wqos and bqos*. 2020. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1707.08365v5) Location: Question 2.15, p. 9; Buriola–Weiermann p. 6.

## Q3373. Collapse absoluteness at higher projective levels

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.4) · **Collection** 34

Does ZFC prove, for every integer n≥5 and infinite cardinal λ, that boldface Σ¹\_n-Coll(ω,λ)-absoluteness implies boldface Σ¹\_n-P-absoluteness for every forcing P of cardinality at most λ?

**Context.** Origin for question 3373: Question first asked by Lukas Koschat, developed in a paper forming part of Straffelini’s doctoral work. Setup for question 3373: For an infinite cardinal λ, Coll(ω,λ) consists of finite partial functions ω→λ, ordered by reverse inclusion. Boldface Σ¹\_n-P-absoluteness means that every Σ¹\_n formula with real parameters from the ground model has the same truth value in that model and every P-generic extension.

**Source.** Cesare Straffelini. *Terminal Absoluteness of Collapse Forcings*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.16330v2) Location: Question 3.4 and §5 conclusion.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3373, checked 8 October 2026: May 2026 v2 retains the unrestricted higher-level question; no later resolution found.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 9.6) · **Collection** 34

Relative to suitable large-cardinal assumptions, is it consistent that ℵ\_ω is strong limit and ℵ\_{ω+1} is measurable in L(P(ℵ\_ω))?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3374: see question 3373. Origin for question 3374: Explicit joint-paper question reached through Straffelini’s doctoral genealogy. ## Q3375. Sharp weight at the distributivity threshold

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 34

Does ZFC prove that there is a sequentially compact space X with w(X)=s which is not h-ssc?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3375, 3376: see question 3373. Origin for questions 3375, 3376: Explicit questions in the joint paper’s final discussion. Setup for questions 3375, 3376: Let s be the splitting number and h the distributivity number of ([ω]^ω,⊆\*); w(X) is minimum base cardinality. A family of spaces is λ-ssc if any λ sequences, each in one family member, share an infinite set of indices along which all converge. A space is λ-ssc when its singleton family is.

**Source.** Sirio Resteghini; Cesare Straffelini. *Simultaneous Sequential Compactness*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15593v1) Location: p. 14, first final question.

<a id="q3380"></a>

## Q3380. Removing the large cardinal from finite-equivalence classification

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 34

Does ZFC prove that T_P is not Borel complete whenever P is minimally unbounded?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3380, 3381: Question in the final primary article. Setup for questions 3380, 3381: Let P be countably infinite with finite principal downsets and δ:P→{2,3,…}. On M=∏\_{p∈P}δ(p), let E_p(x,y) iff x(q)=y(q) for every q≤p; T_P=Th(M;(E_p)). Bounded means δ and chain lengths have a common finite bound. Minimally unbounded means unbounded, but each downward closed Q⊆P has Q or P\Q bounded. Borel completeness means every countable-structure isomorphism problem Borel-reduces to isomorphism on Mod(T_P). Potential cardinality ||T|| counts ground-model sets that become canonical Scott sentences of countable T-models in some forcing extension; it is ∞ for a proper class.

**Source.** Michael C. Laskowski; Danielle S. Ulrich. *Borel complexity of families of finite equivalence relations via large cardinals*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00153-026-01007-5) Location: §10, p. 507.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3380, 3381, checked 8 October 2026: February 2026 final article retains both targets; no later resolution found.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.15) · **Collection** 34

## Q3430. Determinacy of graph-query games

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 7.1) · **Collection** 35

Is G(S) determined for every graph property S?

**Context.** Origin: Joint-paper questions extending Csernák–Soukup’s transfinite graph-query games. Setup: Let E=[N]^2 and let S⊆2^E be invariant under vertex permutations. In G(S), for ω rounds Seeker chooses a previously unqueried pair and Hider answers edge/nonedge. Seeker wins exactly when some pair remains unqueried and all graphs compatible with the answers either belong to S or all lie outside S. S is ∞-elusive when Seeker has no winning strategy; determined means one player has a winning strategy. Supporting sources: https://sites.google.com/view/tamaskatay/introduction

**Source.** Márton Elekes, Tamás Kátay, Anett Kocsis. *Elusive properties of countably infinite graphs*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11798v3) Location: Question 7.1 p. 26; § 2.1.

**Literature check.** Status checked 2026-10-09: October 2025 v3 retains both targets; current author list gives no replacement.

**Further links.** [1](https://sites.google.com/view/tamaskatay/introduction)

## Q3432. CSP dichotomy with dependent choice

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 5.3) · **Collection** 35

Is there a model of ZF+DC in which, simultaneously for every finite D, K_D holds exactly when D admits a cyclic polymorphism?

**Context.** Origin: Explicit joint-paper Problem 5.3. Setup: For finite relational D in a finite signature, K_D asserts: every structure X of that signature maps homomorphically to D whenever all its finite substructures do. A cyclic polymorphism is a homomorphism f:D^m→D, m≥2, invariant under cyclic rotation; relations on powers are coordinatewise. Supporting sources: https://sites.google.com/view/tamaskatay/introduction

**Source.** Tamás Kátay, László Márton Tóth, Zoltán Vidnyánszky. *The CSP Dichotomy, the Axiom of Choice, and Cyclic Polymorphisms*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.00514v2) Location: Problem 5.3 p. 18; Definition 2.1.

<a id="q3435"></a>

## Q3435. Hausdorff homomorphism-distinguishing topology

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 35

Is this topology on 𝒵 Hausdorff?

**Context.** Origin: Explicit topology question in § 5 following Definition 4.5. Setup: Let 𝒢 be isomorphism types of finite simple undirected graphs, and 𝒵⊂𝒢 the nonempty connected ones. Write hom(F,G) for the number of graph homomorphisms. For A⊆𝒢 put G≡\_A H iff hom(F,G)=hom(F,H) for every F∈A, and cl(A)={F∈𝒢: ∀G,H∈𝒢, G≡\_A H⇒hom(F,G)=hom(F,H)}. On 𝒵, declare A closed iff cl(A)∩𝒵=A. Supporting sources: https://tseppelt.github.io/

**Source.** Josse van Dobben de Bruyn, Jérémie Marquès, David E. Roberson, Tim Seppelt, Gian Luca Spitzer, Peter Zeman. *Topological and Geometric Perspectives on Homomorphism Indistinguishability*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07927v1) Location: § 5 p. 11; Definition 4.5 p. 11.

**Literature check.** Status checked 2026-10-09: October 2026 v1 proves T₁, disconnectedness and noncompactness; Hausdorffness remains asked.

**Further links.** [1](https://tseppelt.github.io/)


<a id="q3482"></a>

## Q3482. Subhalting categoricity of linear orders

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.4.14) · **Collection** 35

Do a Turing degree d<0′ and a computable linear order exist that is d-computably categorical but not computably categorical?

**Context.** Origin: Thesis question; related Boolean-algebra analogue is already negative. Setup: A computable linear order has domain N and decidable comparison. It is d-computably categorical if each computable isomorphic copy has an isomorphism computable with an oracle of Turing degree d. Computably categorical means d=0; 0′ is the halting-problem degree.

**Source.** Java Darleen Villano. *Computable Categoricity, and Topology in Reverse Mathematics*. University of Connecticut, 2025. Advisor(s): David Reed Solomon and Damir D. Dzhafarov; associate advisor Karen Lange. [primary source](https://javavillano.github.io/thesisfinal.pdf) Location: Question 2.4.14, p. 74.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: The later Extensions §4.2 rules out the stronger requirement covering all d-computable copies. It does not resolve this computable-copy question; searches found no resolution.

Partial results do not reset counters; software, source and service errors are excluded. The original 30-question membership is preserved. [Report](../../solutions/b1-continuation-2026-10-12/STATUS.json).

## Q3493. Six-element Ramsey choice

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 35

Does ZF prove RC₆⇒LOC₆⁻?

**Context.** Origin: Doctoral open question, retained in Halbeisen–Schumacher’s joint paper. Setup: Work in ZF. RCₙ asserts every infinite set X contains an infinite subset Y with a choice function on its n-element subsets. LOCₙ⁻ asserts every infinite linearly orderable family of n-element sets has an infinite subfamily admitting a choice function.

**Source.** Salome Eva Schumacher. *Sets of range uniqueness, magic sets and weak choice principles*. ETH Zurich, 2021. Advisor(s): Lorenz Halbeisen, Norbert Hungerbühler (co-supervisor). [primary source](https://www.research-collection.ethz.ch/bitstreams/ff770c1e-3454-4fdc-a2e6-f279cd199e55/download) Location: §1.3 Question 1, p. 55; companion §4 Question 2, p. 732.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: the final 2023 companion retains the question. The 2024 Ramsey-choice implication paper and its reference-numbering correction do not resolve it; no later resolution located.

**Literature check.** Status sources: https://doi.org/10.1002/malq.202300024; correction https://doi.org/10.1002/malq.202430002.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00153-022-00860-4) · [2](https://doi.org/10.1002/malq.202300024) · [3](https://doi.org/10.1002/malq.202430002)

## Q3494. The remaining four-cardinal ordering

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 35

Is it consistent with ZF that an infinite set M satisfies |M²|<|[M]²|<|fin(M)|<|seq_inj(M)|?

**Context.** Origin: Residual Diagram C in the joint paper; not verified as a thesis question. Setup: For a set M, fin(M) is its finite subsets, [M]² its two-element subsets, and seq_inj(M) its finite repetition-free sequences. Write |X|<|Y| when X injects into Y but no bijection exists.

**Source.** Lorenz Halbeisen, Riccardo Plati, Salome Schumacher, Saharon Shelah. *Four cardinals and their relations in ZF*. 2023. [primary source](https://shelah.logic.at/files/225337/1-s2.0-S0168007222001154-main.pdf) Location: §1 Diagram C, pp. 2–3; §3, p. 16.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: final 2023 paper proves the other five orders consistent. Comparing Generalised Powers in ZF (January 2026) treats fixed finite arities; no resolution of Diagram C located.

**Further links.** [1](https://shelah.logic.at/files/252048/cgp.pdf)
