## Q3552. Are uncountably many distinct varieties of commutative semilinear residuated lattices amalgamating?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.12) · **Collection** 36

Are uncountably many distinct varieties of commutative semilinear residuated lattices amalgamating?

**Context.** AP means every embedding span A→B,A→C has commuting embeddings into one algebra in the class. A residuated lattice is a lattice/monoid satisfying xy≤z iff y≤x\z iff x≤z/y. Semilinear means a subdirect product of chains; cancellative means both monoid cancellations hold.

**Source.** Wesley Fussner and Simon Santschi. *Amalgamation in Semilinear Residuated Lattices*. Bern mathematics, 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11225-025-10206-9) Location: Question 4.12, p.124.

**Literature check.** Status: No later solution located.

## Q3562. Let GC² be two-variable guarded first-order logic with counting, over finite unary/binary…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 36

Let GC² be two-variable guarded first-order logic with counting, over finite unary/binary relational signatures. GPres² additionally allows local Presburger constraints: integer linear comparisons or congruences between counts of neighbors satisfying formulas, using forward or inverse binary guards. If a GPres² formula is equivalent on finite structures to an ordinary first-order formula, must it be equivalent there to a GC² formula over the same signature? Does this also hold on finitely branching structures, where every binary relation has finite incoming and outgoing neighborhoods?

**Source.** Filippo De Bortoli. *Complexity and Expressive Power of Description Logics with Numerical Constraints*. TU Dresden, 2025. [primary source](https://lat.inf.tu-dresden.de/research/theses/2025/DeBortoli-Diss-2025.pdf) Location: Chapter 10, p.136.

**Literature check.** Status: Ordinary first-order formulas may use arbitrarily many variables; auxiliary predicates are not allowed. The final Baader–De Bortoli FroCoS follow-up and CSL 2026 arithmetic-logic results concern other expressivity/satisfiability questions and do not supply this characterization. No later resolution located October 9, 2026.

## Q3563. Over RCA\*₀, does ADS plus exponential closure of I⁰₁ imply CRT²₂? Ask also with CAC replacing ADS.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.6) · **Collection** 36

Over RCA\*₀, does ADS plus exponential closure of I⁰₁ imply CRT²₂? Ask also with CAC replacing ADS.

**Source.** Kołodziejczyk and Sun. *Advisor follow-up, arXiv:2604.01808v2*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01808v2) Location: Question 3.6, p.10.

**Notes.** RCA\*₀ has bounded induction, exponentiation and Δ⁰₁ comprehension. I⁰₁ is the intersection of Σ⁰₁-definable cuts, allowing set parameters; exponential closure means x∈I⁰₁⇒2^x∈I⁰₁. ADS supplies an unbounded ascending or descending subset of each linear order on N. CAC supplies an unbounded chain or antichain in each partial order. CRT²₂ supplies every two-coloring of pairs an unbounded subset on which each row is eventually constant. This is a new residual question: the paper resolves several earlier dissertation questions. Latest version and later literature checked October 9, 2026; no resolution located.

## Q3567. What is the exact consistency strength over ZFC of a weakly compact κ satisfying b_κ(∈\*)>κ+?

**Literature check.** Status: This residual question follows the paper’s solution of general higher-avoidance separation, which is excluded from this batch. Its current author version and July/October 2026 follow-ups were checked; no resolution located. The question’s >κ+ is explicit; a separate corollary’s >κ typo is not used.

**Notes.** Here b_κ(∈\*) is the least size of F⊆κ^κ for which no slalom φ eventually captures every f∈F, where φ(α)⊆κ and |φ(α)|≤|α| for infinite α<κ. Capture means f(α)∈φ(α) outside a bounded subset. Weak compactness means uncountable inaccessibility and κ→(κ)^2_2.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.3.4) · **Collection** 37

Which α<δ¹₂ occur as the least ordinal with D(α) ill-founded for a recursive pseudodilator D? A predilator is an endofunctor on linear orders/order-embeddings preserving direct limits, pullbacks and pointwise domination; recursive means computably coded on finite orders. **Source.** Hanul Jeon. *Proof theory for higher pointclasses*. Cornell University, 2026. Advisor(s): Justin Moore and James Walsh. [primary source](https://hanuljeon95.github.io/files/PhD_Thesis.pdf) Location: Question 1.3.4, p.57.

## Q3663. Is every 1-generic A⊆N dispersive? Put δ(X,Y)=limsup_n |(X△Y)∩[0,n)|/n and…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 37

Is every 1-generic A⊆N dispersive? Put δ(X,Y)=limsup_n |(X△Y)∩[0,n)|/n and H(a,b)=max{sup_{X∈a}inf_{Y∈b}δ(X,Y),sup_{Y∈b}inf_{X∈a}δ(X,Y)} for Turing degrees. Dispersive means H(deg_T A,deg_T B)=1 for almost every B under fair-coin measure. One-generic means meeting or avoiding every computably enumerable set S of binary strings: some initial segment lies in S, or some initial segment has no extension in S.

**Source.** Tiago Royer. *Kolmogorov Complexity of Attractive Degrees*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.02726) Location: §2.1, p.6.

## Q3664. For every k≥3, are there ε>0 and arbitrarily large existential-positive FO^{k+1} sentences φ…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 37

For every k≥3, are there ε>0 and arbitrarily large existential-positive FO^{k+1} sentences φ having an equivalent existential-positive FO^k sentence, but every such equivalent sentence has size ≥2^{ε|φ|}? Equivalence is over all structures in the same finite signature.

**Context.** FO^k permits k reusable variables; its existential-positive fragment uses atoms, equality, ∧,∨,∃. Formula size counts syntax-tree symbols.

**Source.** Harry Vinall-Smeeth. *How Small Can You Go? Lower Bounds on Representations in Knowledge Compilation, Finite Model Theory, and Beyond*. Humboldt-Universität zu Berlin, 2026. Advisor(s): Christoph Berkholz. [primary source](https://edoc.hu-berlin.de/items/c8b26866-fb73-4f4f-ba7d-e5bc583203fd) Location: Chapter 13, p.186; Theorem 11.20.

**Literature check.** Status: Neither question imposes a globally fixed signature. The dissertation reaffirms these residual questions after the earlier paper; later work checked October 9, 2026, with no resolution located.

<a id="q3754"></a>

## Q3754. Effective descent for finitely presented Heyting algebras

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 8.1.7) · **Collection** 38

Let C be the opposite of the category of Heyting algebras with finite generators and relations, and Heyting homomorphisms. Is every regular epimorphism e:B→A in C an effective descent morphism? Regular epimorphism means a coequalizer of a pair. Effective descent means that the pullback functor e\*:C/A→C/B is monadic: its canonical comparison to algebras for the monad e\*Σ\_e is an equivalence, where Σ\_e composes arrows with e.

**Context.** Origin: Thesis problems. Setup: A Heyting algebra is a bounded distributive lattice with implication characterized by x≤a→b iff a∧x≤b.

**Source.** Rodrigo Nicolau Almeida. *Representations and model theory of free Heyting algebras*. University of Amsterdam, 2026. Advisor(s): Nick Bezhanishvili and Mamuka Jibladze. [primary source](https://eprints.illc.uva.nl/id/eprint/2448/1/DS-2026-14.text.pdf) Location: Problem 8.1.7, p.263.

## Q3757. Nonpolyhedral semialgebraic modal logic

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 38

Is there a closed semialgebraic X⊆Rⁿ whose semialgebraic modal logic is not the polyhedral modal logic of any compact polyhedron? For X, variables range over semialgebraic subsets, Boolean operations are set operations, and ◇ is relative Euclidean closure. For a compact polyhedron P (a finite union of simplices), variables instead range over subsets of P defined by Boolean combinations of linear inequalities. Each logic consists of formulas evaluating to the whole space under every such valuation.

**Context.** Origin: Thesis question.

**Source.** Simon Kreuzer. *Modal Logics of Tame Topologies*. University of Amsterdam, 2026. [primary source](https://eprints.illc.uva.nl/id/eprint/2416/1/MoL-2026-04.text.pdf) Location: After Corollary 5.65, p.79.

## Q3758. Rapid constructible Raisonnier filters and domination

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 7.1) · **Collection** 38

Does ∀a∈ω^ω(F_(2^ω∩L[a]) is rapid) imply ∀a∈ω^ω∃d∈ω^ω∀f∈L[a]∩ω^ω(f≤\*d), where f≤\*d means eventual pointwise domination?

**Context.** Origin: Questions in the later student–advisor paper. Setup: For X⊆2^ω, put H(Y)={min{n:x(n)≠y(n)}:x,y∈Y,x≠y}. Let F_X contain a⊆ω iff X has a countable cover (Y_n) with ⋃\_nH(Y_n)⊆a. A filter F is rapid if, for every increasing f:ω→ω, some a∈F satisfies |a∩[0,f(n))|≤n for all n. Put R={X:F_X is rapid}. L[a] is the constructible universe relative to a.

**Source.** Spyridon Dialiatsis and Yurii Khomskii. *Combinatorial Properties of the Raisonnier Filter*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.23340v1) Location: Question 7.1, p.14.

**Literature check.** Status: Latest version v1 checked 9 October 2026; questions remain explicit.

**Further links.** [1](https://eprints.illc.uva.nl/id/eprint/2399/)

## Q3763. Decidability of admissibility in K

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 38

Is there an algorithm deciding, for finite sets Γ,Δ of modal formulas, whether every substitution σ satisfying K⊢σγ for all γ∈Γ also satisfies K⊢σδ for some δ∈Δ?

**Context.** Origin: Longstanding problems restated. Setup: K is the normal unimodal logic of all Kripke frames; K4 adds □p→□□p. NExt(K4) is the inclusion-ordered lattice of normal extensions. A splitting is L for which some M satisfies M⊈L and, for every N, M⊆N or N⊆L. A union-splitting is a join of splittings. ## Q3767. Pointed AKE for imperfect deeply ramified fields

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.7) · **Collection** 38

Fix prime p and e∈N_{>0}∪{∞}. Take henselian (K,v,t),(L,w,s) of characteristic p and imperfection degree e, with perfect completions and 0&lt;v(t),w(s)<∞. Assume their coarsenings O_v[1/t],O_w[1/s] are separably tame. Does (vK,v(t))≡(wL,w(s)) together with O_v/(t)≡O_w/(s) imply (K,v,t)≡(L,w,s)? Here [K:K^p]=p^e; e=∞ means infinite degree. Separably tame means trivially valued, or having p-divisible value group, perfect residue field and no proper immediate separable algebraic extension; immediate preserves residue field and value group.

## Q3822. Unique multipartite endomorphism topology

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.4.9) · **Collection** 39

For K on N×N with (i,a) adjacent to (j,b) exactly when i≠j, is pointwise topology the unique Polish semigroup topology on End(K)?

**Context.** For countable relational A, End(A) comprises relation-preserving self-maps. Pointwise topology is inherited from A^A with A discrete. Semigroup topologies make composition jointly continuous; Polish means separable and completely metrizable. Here ω-categorical means Aut(A) has finitely many orbits on A^n for every n. Origin/locators: Q3822: Schindler thesis Question 5.4.9, p.89. Both: Marimon–Pinsker, A guide to topological reconstruction on endomorphism monoids and polymorphism clones, Questions 5.18,5.7, pp.19,17; Q3823 is later-paper-origin.

**Source.** Paolo Marimon and Michael Pinsker. *A guide to topological reconstruction on endomorphism monoids and polymorphism clones*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.01086v2) Location: Schindler thesis Question 5.4.9, p.89; Marimon–Pinsker, A guide to topological reconstruction on endomorphism monoids and polymorphism clones, Questions 5.18 and 5.7, pp.19,17

## Q3860. Do there exist 2-random reals x<\_T z and a constant c such that |K(x↾n)−K(z↾n)|≤c for every n?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 1.4) · **Collection** 39

Do there exist 2-random reals x<\_T z and a constant c such that |K(x↾n)−K(z↾n)|≤c for every n?

**Context.** Paper-origin problem contributed by George Barmpalias to Open Problems in Mathematical Logic, 2026 Tianyuan Workshop in Definability and Computation; arXiv:2608.26628v2, 31 August 2026, Problem 1.4(2), p.3. Setup: A real is an infinite binary sequence. It is 2-random when Martin-Löf random relative to the halting set. K denotes prefix-free Kolmogorov complexity for a fixed universal prefix-free machine; x↾n is the first n bits. The notation x<\_T z means x is Turing-computable from z but z is not Turing-computable from x.

**Source.** George Barmpalias, Su Gao, Jialiang He, Takayuki Kihara, Andre Nies, Theodore Slaman, Chieu-Minh Tran, Daniel Turetsky, Philip Welch, Liang Yu, Hang Zhang. *Open Problems in Mathematical Logic*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26628v2) Location: Problem 1.4(2), p.3.

**Literature check.** Status: The current workshop version explicitly asks this; searches through 10 October 2026 found no matching resolution. The same-jump question is a different part of Problem 1.4 and is not repeated here.

## Q3865. If A is homogeneous in a finite relational language, does F[A^d] have finite length for every…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.2) · **Collection** 39

If A is homogeneous in a finite relational language, does F[A^d] have finite length for every field F and d≥1? Homogeneous means finite induced-substructure isomorphisms extend to automorphisms.

**Context.** Doctoral route: Arka Ghosh, Linear Algebra in Orbit-finite Dimension, University of Warsaw doctorate awarded 27 November 2025; supervisor Sławomir Lasota, auxiliary supervisor Piotr Hofman. His Chapter 8 discusses the weaker Noetherian question. Primary formulations: David M. Evans, Permutation modules for Ramsey structures, arXiv:2603.29606v2 (9 September 2026), Question 1.2, p.2; Jingjie Yang, Mikołaj Bojańczyk and Bartek Klin, The Finite Length Property of the Rado Graph and Friends, LICS 2026, §9, p.82:25. Q3865 is a restated question; Q3866 is explicitly newly asked there. Setup: A is countable, G=Aut(A), and F[A^d] is the free F-vector space on A^d with coordinatewise G-action. Finite length means a finite bound on lengths of strictly nested chains of G-invariant subspaces.

**Source.** David M. Evans. *Permutation modules for Ramsey structures*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.29606v2) Location: Question 1.2, p.2; Jingjie Yang, Mikołaj Bojańczyk and Bartek Klin, The Finite Length Property of the Rado Graph and Friends, LICS 2026, §9, p.82:25.

**Literature check.** Status: No resolution found through 10 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.mimuw.edu.pl/en/doctorates/arka-ghosh/) · [2](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.LICS.2026.82)

## Q4001. Is derivability decidable in its fragment whose formulas are built only from propositional…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 41

Is derivability decidable in its fragment whose formulas are built only from propositional variables, the left implication →, and !^s?

**Context.** Take the intuitionistic calculus acLL_Σ of that paper, with exactly one label s and f(s)={C,W,E,A1,A2}. Antecedents are ordered binary trees, with empty subtrees erased. Sequents have one succedent formula; no global structural rules apply. The modality !^s has dereliction and promotion, and licenses weakening, nonlocal contraction, exchange and both specified associativity rules, exactly as in Figures 1–2; contraction can identify separated copies of an entirely !^s-marked subtree. Degree award was not independently established. Origin: The dissertation restates the joint paper’s minimal-fragment question. No resolution found through 10 October 2026. Undecidability with additives, or with a second associativity-only modality, does not settle this fragment. The 2026 Galatos–Sangalli deducibility result also uses additive join.

**Source.** Eben Blaisdell. *Subexponentials in Nonassociative Lambek Calculus*. University of Pennsylvania, 2024. Advisor(s): Andre Scedrov. [primary source](https://repository.upenn.edu/bitstreams/c318a63b-b658-45b1-a8d3-ff6378032ab1/download) Location: Thesis §6.2.1, page 70; earlier IJCAR 2022 paper by Blaisdell, Max Kanovich, Stepan L. Kuznetsov, Elaine Pimentel and Scedrov, §§3–4.

**Literature check.** Status: Origin: The dissertation restates the joint paper’s minimal-fragment question. No resolution found through 10 October 2026. Undecidability with additives, or with a second associativity-only modality, does not settle this fragment. The 2026 Galatos–Sangalli deducibility result also uses additive join.

<a id="q4044"></a>

## Q4044. Is group isomorphism on finitely generated bi-orderable groups universal?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.1) · **Collection** 41

Is group isomorphism on finitely generated bi-orderable groups universal?

**Context.** Represent finitely generated groups by normal subgroups N of the countably generated free group F∞ containing all but finitely many basis elements, with the inherited Borel structure from 2^{F∞}. Restrict to the indicated quotient groups F∞/N. Left/bi-orderability means existence of a total order invariant under left/both-sided multiplication. Amenability means existence of a left-invariant mean on bounded real functions. A countable Borel equivalence relation E is universal if every countable Borel equivalence relation F admits a Borel map f with xFy⇔f(x)Ef(y). Weak universality instead requires a countable-to-one Borel map with xFy⇒f(x)Ef(y). Origin: Paper questions. Status: Latest arXiv v2 and exact final journal formulations checked; no later resolution found, 10 October 2026.

**Source.** Filippo Calderoni and Adam Clay. *The isomorphism problem for finitely generated bi-orderable groups*. 2027. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jalgebra.2026.07.005) Location: Question 4.1

**Literature check.** Status: Origin: Paper questions. Status: Latest arXiv v2 and exact final journal formulations checked; no later resolution found, 10 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.10673v2)

<a id="q4046"></a>

## Q4046. Does every computable bi-orderable metabelian group have a computable copy admitting a…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 41

Does every computable bi-orderable metabelian group have a computable copy admitting a computable bi-order? Here metabelian means its commutator subgroup is abelian.

**Context.** A computable group has computable domain and multiplication; a computable copy is an isomorphic computable group. A bi-order is a two-sided invariant total order; computable orders have decidable comparison. Put X(G)={positive cones of bi-orders}, topologized inside {0,1}^G. Its Cantor–Bendixson rank is the first stage at which iteratively deleting isolated points stabilizes; limit stages take intersections. A computable ordinal has a computable well-order presentation.

**Source.** Waseet Kazmi. *Ordered Groups, Computability and Cantor-Bendixson Rank*. University of Connecticut, 2023. Advisor(s): David Reed Solomon. [primary source](https://www2.math.uconn.edu/~solomon/WaseetKazmi_Dissertation_Final.pdf) Location: Origin: Arman Darbinyan, Computability, orders, and solvable groups, Question 1; restated in the thesis.

**Literature check.** Status checked 10 October 2026: no resolution found; the later finite-Cantor–Bendixson-rank construction does not impose the computability condition.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/1909.05720)

<a id="q4132"></a>

## Q4132. Do such A,T exist for every x that is neither Schnorr random nor weakly 1-generic, with T…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 42

Do such A,T exist for every x that is neither Schnorr random nor weakly 1-generic, with T additionally ergodic?

**Context.** On Cantor space 2^N use fair-coin measure λ. Effectively open means a computably enumerable union of cylinders; Π⁰₁ means effectively closed. A Schnorr test is a uniformly effectively open sequence (Uₙ) with uniformly computable measures λ(Uₙ)≤2^(−n); Schnorr-random points avoid every such intersection. Weakly 1-generic points belong to every dense effectively open set; 1-generic points avoid the boundary of every effectively open set. A total map T is computable if cylinder preimages are uniformly effectively open. Seek a Π⁰₁ set A containing x with positive computable λ(A), and a total computable λ-preserving T:2^N→2^N with Tᵏ(x)∉A for every k≥1.

**Source.** Joey Veltri. *Effective recurrence for computable measure-preserving transformations*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12402v1) Location: Questions 3.50–3.51, pp. 40–41.

**Literature check.** Status: These are paper-origin questions. The larger point class in Q4133 and stronger dynamical requirement in Q4132 prevent either from being an immediate weaker formulation of the other. No matching resolution found, 10 October 2026.

<a id="q4182"></a>

## Q4182. For each n≥2, what is the exact computability-theoretic complexity of CP3(nV)?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 7.7) · **Collection** 42

For each n≥2, what is the exact computability-theoretic complexity of CP3(nV)?

**Context.** Let C={0,1}^N. Brin's group nV consists of homeomorphisms of C^n given by finite coordinatewise prefix replacements between partitions into product cylinders. Inputs can be finite prefix tables, or equivalently words in a fixed finite generating set. Define CP3(nV) as the set of pairs (f,g) for which some h∈Homeo(C^n) satisfies hfh⁻¹=g; h need not belong to nV.

**Source.** Ville Salo. *Conjugacy problems in higher-dimensional Thompson groups*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11124v1) Location: Question 7.7, p. 16.

**Literature check.** Status checked 10 October 2026: The current paper proves undecidability and gives an analytic Σ¹₁ upper bound, but no sharper upper bound. Its Σ⁰₁-completeness results concern different conjugacy problems. No later resolution was found.

## Q4323. Is there an algorithm deciding whether such a sentence has a nonempty model, finite or infinite?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 44

Is there an algorithm deciding whether such a sentence has a nonempty model, finite or infinite?

**Context.** An input is a finite first-order sentence with equality over a finite unary/binary relational signature, no constants or function symbols, and only two reusable variable names. One distinguished binary predicate < must be interpreted as a strict partial order, meaning transitive and irreflexive; the other predicates are unrestricted.

**Source.** Dariusz Marzec and Lidia Tendera. *On two-variable first-order logic with a partial order*. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1804.09447) Location: §2.2 and final Discussion.

**Literature check.** Status checked 11 October2026: the final retains this question. The authors of a2013 general decidability claim acknowledged its failure in2018; finite-model decidability and restricted-fragment results do not settle this target.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1093/logcom/exaf068)

## Q4420. For every g∈F(P)\H, must some homomorphism φ from F(P) to a finite nilpotent group satisfy…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 45

For every g∈F(P)\H, must some homomorphism φ from F(P) to a finite nilpotent group satisfy φ(g)∉φ(H)? Equivalently, is H closed in the pro-nilpotent topology?

**Context.** Genealogy: Etedadialiabadi's Generic behaviour of a measure preserving transformation, Illinois Urbana-Champaign PhD, December2017; research director Slawomir Solecki. This is background, not the question's origin. Setup: Let X be a finite nonempty set and P a set of bijections between subsets of X. Assume the undirected graph joining x to p(x), whenever defined, is connected. Fix x0∈X. In the free group F(P), let H consist of reduced words whose successive partial-permutation actions are defined at x0 and return it to x0. Suppose the maps in P have pairwise distinct permutation extensions on a common finite superset of X, generating a nilpotent group.

**Source.** Mahmood Etedadialiabadi and Su Gao. *Extension properties for partial permutations*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04870v1) Location: Problem4.31, p.37; definitions §§4.1,4.4.

**Literature check.** Status: Posed in the current version; no resolution located through11October2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://hdl.handle.net/2142/99247)

## Q4503. For every n>1, does T_n have a model companion if and only if every finitely generated group…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1) · **Collection** 46

For every n>1, does T_n have a model companion if and only if every finitely generated group satisfying x^n=1 is finite?

**Context.** DL-S05 — Model theory / group theory Primary-paper source: Yawara Ishida, Ryosuke Mizuno and Kota Takeuchi, Existence of a Model Companion for Groups of Exponent 3, arXiv:2609.30061v1 (24 September 2026), Conjecture 1.1, p.2. https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30061 Origin: Conjecture attributed explicitly to Takeuchi; the paper notes his earlier 2022 discussion of the connection. No doctoral attribution. Setup: For each integer n>1, T_n is the group theory with identity x^n=1, so exponents divide n. A model companion T_n\* is model-complete, and every model of either theory embeds into a model of the other; model-complete means every embedding between its models is elementary.

**Source.** Yawara Ishida, Ryosuke Mizuno and Kota Takeuchi. *Existence of a Model Companion for Groups of Exponent 3*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30061v1) Location: Conjecture 1.1, p.2.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30061)

## Q4504. Must there be a definable equivalence relation E on M with infinitely many classes and a…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.7) · **Collection** 46

Must there be a definable equivalence relation E on M with infinitely many classes and a definable binary relation R on M inducing a strict linear order on M/E? Both relations may use parameters; the interpretation domain is M itself.

**Context.** DL-S06 — Model theory / definable orders Primary-paper source: Grzegorz Jagiella, On canonicity of almost linear minimal orders, arXiv:2609.36313v2 (30 September 2026), Question 2.7, p.5; interpretation convention §§1.1–1.3. https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36313 Origin: Tanović's earlier question, explicitly retained here; no doctoral attribution. Setup: (M,<) is an infinite partially ordered structure, with < irreflexive and transitive, containing an infinite chain. It is minimal: every subset of M definable using parameters is finite or cofinite.

**Source.** Grzegorz Jagiella. *On canonicity of almost linear minimal orders*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36313v2) Location: Question 2.7, p.5; interpretation convention §§1.1–1.3.

## Q4522. Is T(C_n,H)<∞ for every n≥2, where C_n is the cyclic group of order n?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 46

Is T(C_n,H)<∞ for every n≥2, where C_n is the cyclic group of order n?

**Context.** Setup: H is the countable locally finite universal homogeneous group: it contains every finite group, and every isomorphism between finite subgroups extends to an automorphism. Emb(A,B) denotes injective group homomorphisms. Define T(A,H) as the least positive integer t such that every finite coloring χ of Emb(A,H) admits w∈Emb(H,H) with |χ(w∘Emb(A,H))|≤t; put T(A,H)=∞ if none exists. Checked 11 October 2026: current v3 retains this cyclic-group target while proving infinite degrees for noncyclic finite abelian groups; no later resolution located.

**Source.** Dragan Mašulović and Veljko Toljić. *Hall’s universal group does not have finite big Ramsey degrees*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23618) Location: Open problem 1, p.11; definitions pp.2–3.

**Literature check.** Status: Checked 11 October 2026: current v3 retains this cyclic-group target while proving infinite degrees for noncyclic finite abelian groups; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23618v3)


<a id="q4578"></a>

## Q4578. If B is connected and bisoluble with left chief length three, must it be strongly left soluble,…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.1) · **Collection** 46

If B is connected and bisoluble with left chief length three, must it be strongly left soluble, or even soluble?

**Context.** Setup: B=(B,+,∘) has finite Morley rank, two group laws and a∘(b+c)=a∘b−a+a∘c. It is connected if (B,+) has no proper definable finite-index subgroup, and bisoluble if both groups are soluble. Put λ\_a(b)=−a+a∘b and a\*b=λ\_a(b)−b. A strong left ideal is an additive normal subgroup invariant under every λ\_a; an ideal is additionally multiplicatively normal. Weak/strong-left/ordinary solubility means a finite series B=L₀≥⋯≥L_s=0 of subbraces/strong-left-ideals/ideals, respectively, with [L_i,L_i]\_+ and L_i\*L_i contained in L_{i+1}; subbraces are subgroups for both laws. Left chief length is the length of a maximal chain of definable connected strong left ideals from B to 0. Checked 11 October 2026: current primary retains both targets; bounded-rank results do not settle them.

**Source.** Maria Ferrara, Moreno Invitti, Marco Trombetti and Frank Olaf Wagner. *Bisoluble and Binilpotent Skew Braces of Finite Morley Rank*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27999) Location: Question 6.1, p.28.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27999v1)

**Context.** Setup: In Buss’s bounded arithmetic, S₂ⁱ=BASIC+Σᵇᵢ-length-induction and T₂ⁱ=BASIC+Σᵇᵢ-induction. BB(Γ) is the sharply bounded replacement scheme (∀j≤|x| ∃y≤t φ(j,y))⇒(∃w ∀j≤|x| φ(j,(w)ⱼ)), for φ∈Γ; w codes a sequence and |x| is binary length. ## Q4651. Does Ab+(Arch) equal ⊨R?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 47

Does Ab+(Arch) equal ⊨R?

**Context.** Setup: Use countably-many variables; N={0,1,…}. Ab is the finitary logic of ordered additive reals: a→b is interpreted as b−a, & as addition, ∧ minimum, ∨ maximum, t=0, with nonnegative designated values. Let ⊨A denote consequence from arbitrary premise sets under valuations into A=R or Z. Put n·φ=φ&⋯&φ, 0·φ=t. Rule (Arch) is {ψ→n·φ:n∈N}▶φ; (IDC) is {2·φₙ₊₁→φₙ,φₙ→4·φₙ₊₁:n∈N}▶φ₀→t. For any rule Γ▶φ, its ∨-form comprises {γ∨χ:γ∈Γ}▶φ∨χ for every formula χ. Origins: Cintula–Jankovec–Noguera, Superabelian logics, final p229 after Theorem3.4 and Conjecture3.8 p231; retained in thesis pp20,22. Audited provenance and status: Subject: Mathematical logic Publisher final 19(2):218–244, June2026; latest arXiv v5 April2026. Thesis title-page distinguishes supervisor Cintula and advisor Noguera.

**Source.** Petr Cintula, Filip Jankovec and Carles Noguera. *Superabelian logics*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.20170v5) Location: Cintula–Jankovec–Noguera, Superabelian logics, final p229 after Theorem3.4 and Conjecture3.8 p231; retained in thesis pp20,22.

**Literature check.** Status: Final and September2026 thesis retain both questions; checked 12 October 2026, no matching resolution located.

## Q4686. Is it consistent with ZFC that an entangled set exists and every 2-entangled subset of R is…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 0.6) · **Collection** 47

Is it consistent with ZFC that an entangled set exists and every 2-entangled subset of R is entangled?

**Context.** Setup: An uncountable E⊆R is n-entangled if, for every τ:n→{0,1} and every uncountable family A of pairwise-disjoint n-element subsets of E, some distinct a,b∈A satisfy a(i)&lt;b(i) iff τ(i)=0 for every i&lt;n; a,b are increasingly enumerated. Entangled means n-entangled for every integer n≥1. ## Q4692. Is there a poset (Σ,≤) such that, for every complete consistent first-order T, T has TP exactly…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.4) · **Collection** 47

Is there a poset (Σ,≤) such that, for every complete consistent first-order T, T has TP exactly when some model M⊨T has a formula φ(x;y) and tuples (b_s)\_{s∈Σ} satisfying M⊨∀x(φ(x;b_s)→φ(x;b_t)) iff s≤t?

**Context.** Setup: A complete consistent first-order theory T has TP if some formula θ(x;y), integer k≥2 and tuples (a_η)\_{η∈ω^{<ω}} in a model satisfy: along each infinite branch, the corresponding θ-instances are consistent; at each node η, every k distinct members of {θ(x;a_{η⌢i}):i<ω} are inconsistent. Variables and parameters are finite tuples. Audited provenance and status: Subject: Model theory Thesis unlocated; paper postdates Spring doctorate. Colored-tree equivalences concern instability, TP₁ and IP; no TP poset characterization is stated. Accepted manuscript listed; published final unlocated.

**Source.** Gabriel Day and Scott Mutchnik. *On the notion of a patterning property in model theory*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18533v2) Location: García–Mennuni, Question 4.4(1), restated in Day–Mutchnik, June 2026 v2, Question 3, p27.

**Literature check.** Status: Chernikov’s August 2026 Theorem 1.1 proves SOP₂=SOP₃, settling Day–Mutchnik Question 1. These remain strictly stronger than TP; Question 3 is not resolved.

## Q4698. Is it consistent with ZFC that some κ⁺-supercompact cardinal κ admits a normal ideal I on κ⁺…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 47

Is it consistent with ZFC that some κ⁺-supercompact cardinal κ admits a normal ideal I on κ⁺ with a dense family D of cardinality κ⁺?

**Context.** Setup: A cardinal κ is κ⁺-supercompact if there is an elementary embedding j:V→M into a transitive class with critical point κ, j(κ)>κ⁺ and M closed under κ⁺-sequences. A normal ideal I on κ⁺ is proper, contains every bounded subset, and is closed under diagonal unions {α<κ⁺:∃ξ<α(α∈A_ξ)} for sequences (A_ξ)\_{ξ<κ⁺} in I. Put I⁺=P(κ⁺)∖I and A≤\_I B iff A∖B∈I. A family D⊆I⁺ is dense if every A∈I⁺ has B∈D with B≤\_I A. Audited provenance and status: Subject: Set theory Original October2025 v1 Question2.23 p12; final Question3.23 p12, 19May2026. Dense ideals, latest v1: Main Theorem constructs regular-successor ideals but does not assert preservation of predecessor supercompactness. https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.14359 https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.14359v1

**Source.** Tom Benhamou and Ben-Zion Weltsch. *Supercompact Measures and the Galvin Property*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/jsl.2026.10216) Location: Benhamou–Weltsch, Supercompact Measures and the Galvin Property, May2026 publisher final, Question3.23; density footnote3.

## Q4699. Is it consistent with ZFC that such κ,U admit a projection P_U→Add(κ⁺,1)?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 47

Is it consistent with ZFC that such κ,U admit a projection P_U→Add(κ⁺,1)?

**Context.** Setup: Let κ be an uncountable cardinal and U a κ-complete fine normal ultrafilter on Pκ(κ⁺)={x⊆κ⁺:|x|<κ}. Fine means {x:α∈x}∈U for α<κ⁺; normal means closure under diagonal intersections {x:∀α∈x,x∈A_α}, with all A_α∈U. Write x≺y when x⊆y and |x|<|κ∩y|. Supercompact Prikry conditions (s,A) have finite ≺-increasing stem s, A∈U above its last element; strengthening end-extends s using A and shrinks A. A projection π:P_U→Q is order-preserving, sends 1 to 1, and satisfies ∀p∀q≤π(p)∃p′≤p(π(p′)≤q). Add(κ⁺,1) consists of partial functions κ⁺→2 of size <κ⁺; Col(κ,κ⁺) consists of partial functions κ→κ⁺ of size <κ, both ordered by extension. Origin: Benhamou–Thei–Weltsch, arXiv2605.09161v2, Question6.6 p38; setup §§2.1,2.3. Checked12October2026; no matching resolution located. Audited provenance and status: Subject: Set theory Latest arXiv v2: 3 June2026. Author PDF retains Question6.6 p37.

**Source.** Tom Benhamou, S. Thei and Ben-Zion Weltsch. *On the Intermediate Models of Strongly Compact Prikry Forcing*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09161v2) Location: Benhamou–Thei–Weltsch, arXiv2605.09161v2, Question6.6 p38; setup §§2.1,2.3.

**Literature check.** Status: Origin: Benhamou–Thei–Weltsch, arXiv2605.09161v2, Question6.6 p38; setup §§2.1,2.3. Checked12October2026; no matching resolution located.

## Q4717. Are ZFC+“some such λ has λ-PSP for every lightface λ-Π¹₁ set” and ZFC+“a measurable cardinal…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 48

Are ZFC+“some such λ has λ-PSP for every lightface λ-Π¹₁ set” and ZFC+“a measurable cardinal exists” equiconsistent?

**Context.** Setup: For uncountable strong-limit λ with cf(λ)=ω, give 2^λ the bounded topology [s]={x:s⊆x}, s∈2^α, α<λ. A⊆2^λ is lightface λ-Π¹₁ if A={x:∀y∈2^λ (H_λ,∈,x,y)⊨φ}, with φ first-order and no other parameters; x,y are predicates. H_λ contains sets of hereditary size<λ. A has λ-PSP if |A|≤λ or A contains a closed-in-2^λ copy of 2^λ. Audited provenance and status: Subject: Set theory Doctoral title: Some Contributions to Generalised Descriptive Set Theory and to the Study of the Interplay between Modal Logic and Set Theory. Proposition2.8 quantifies over H_λ; preceding λ-only prose conflicts.

**Source.** Fernando José Barrera Esteban; Vincenzo Dimonte; Sandra Müller. *The λ-PSP at λ-Π^1_1 sets*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15675v2) Location: Fernando José Barrera Esteban: Udine PhD2025; advisor Vincenzo Dimonte.

## Q4723. Over RCA₀, is this principle equivalent to ATR₀, the system of arithmetical transfinite recursion?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 48

Over RCA₀, is this principle equivalent to ATR₀, the system of arithmetical transfinite recursion?

**Context.** Gian Marco Osso: current Udine doctoral student, cycleXXXIX; supervisor Alberto Marcone. Setup: A Laver tree with empty stem is a nonempty prefix-closed T⊆ω^{<ω} such that every node has infinitely many immediate successors. Write [T]={x∈ω^ω:∀n x↾n∈T}. The open Laver partition principle states: every coded open U⊆ω^ω admits such T with [T]⊆U or [T]∩U=∅. ## Q4742. For every admissible α, is there an injective group homomorphism Aut(D_(αe))→Aut(D_α)?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 8.1.10) · **Collection** 48

For every admissible α, is there an injective group homomorphism Aut(D_(αe))→Aut(D_α)?

**Context.** Setup: Let α≥ω be admissible: α is a limit ordinal and L_α satisfies Σ₁-collection. Fix a standard Σ₁-effective indexing (K_γ)\_(γ<α) of subsets of α belonging to L_α. For A,B⊆α, write A≤\_(αe)B if some Σ₁(L_α) relation W⊆α² satisfies ∀γ<α[K_γ⊆A ⇔ ∃δ<α((γ,δ)∈W ∧ K_δ⊆B)]. Definability allows L_α parameters. Put A⊕B={2·ξ:ξ∈A}∪{2·ξ+1:ξ∈B}, using ordinal arithmetic, and A≤\_αB iff A⊕(α∖A)≤\_(αe)B⊕(α∖B). Let D_(αe),D_α be the corresponding partial orders of mutual-reducibility classes. Aut denotes the order-automorphism group. Audited provenance and status: Subject: Higher computability theory Embedding Theorem for the automorphism group of the α-enumeration degrees, final thesis2019. Question8.1.10/Conjecture8.1.11 p155; definitions pp21,25,27,46,48,64. Checked12October2026: no full transfinite resolution located. The thesis proves embedding for regular cardinals under V=L. September2026 ordinary-Turing rigidity claim addresses only the α=ω case of the second target, not all regular cardinals.

**Source.** Dávid Natingga. *Embedding Theorem for the automorphism group of the α-enumeration degrees*. University of Leeds, 2019. Advisor(s): Barry Cooper; Michael Rathjen. [primary source](https://etheses.whiterose.ac.uk/id/eprint/25517/) Location: Thesis Question 8.1.10, p.155.

**Literature check.** Status: Checked12October2026: no full transfinite resolution located. The thesis proves embedding for regular cardinals under V=L. September2026 ordinary-Turing rigidity claim addresses only the α=ω case of the second target, not all regular cardinals.

## Q4745. Is there an algorithm deciding, given K and a concept C₀, whether some minimal K-model I…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 48

Is there an algorithm deciding, given K and a concept C₀, whether some minimal K-model I satisfies C₀ᴵ≠∅?

**Context.** Federica Di Stefano: TU Wien PhDJanuary2026; advisor Mantas Šimkus, co-advisor Magdalena Ortiz. Setup: Roles R are binary relation symbols or their converses. Concepts are Boolean combinations of ⊤, unary predicates, and ∃R.⊤. A finite DL-Liteᴴ\_Bool knowledge base K consists of inclusions C⊑D and R⊑S and assertions C(a),r(a,b). Interpretations have arbitrary nonempty domains and distinct interpretations of individual names. A K-model I is minimal if no K-model J with the same domain and individual interpretations shrinks every unary/binary predicate occurring in K, with at least one strict inclusion. Audited provenance and status: Subject: Description logic and decidability Non-monotonic Description Logics Based on Predicate Minimization: cover5November2025, repository2026. Conjecture §3.6 p118; definitions pp25–32,45–48. KR2025 final §6 p279 retains the broader minimal-model decidability direction. Checked12October2026: no matching resolution located; current author publication list inspected. Thesis is the explicit role-inclusion formulation.

## Q4805. Everywhere Borel matching from a connected toast

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.1.6) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13

Does every d-regular bipartite Borel graph, for finite d≥1, that admits a connected toast have a Borel perfect matching on its entire vertex set? A connected toast is a Borel family T of finite vertex sets with ∪\_{K∈T}E(K)=E(G); for distinct K,L∈T, either B₁(K)∩L=∅, B₁(K)⊆L, or B₁(L)⊆K; and each induced graph on K minus the union of the proper T-subsets of K is connected. Here B₁(K) is the closed graph neighborhood (Definition 3.2.2).

**Source.** Antoine Poulin. *A walk through the measurable arboretum*. McGill University, 2026. [primary source](https://mcgill.scholaris.ca/server/api/core/bitstreams/b2a18854-3768-498d-be32-2c314563b3c8/content) · [record](https://mcgill.scholaris.ca/handle/20.500.14905/141069) Location: Question 3.1.6, printed p. 19 / PDF p. 26; Definition 3.2.2, §3.2. Paper counterpart: Question 2 in arXiv:2210.14300.

**Literature check.** Reviewed 2026-10-13: The thesis and published-author text retain the everywhere question. Measurable/generic matching results and the newer measurable spanning-tree theorem do not give an everywhere Borel perfect matching. No exact closure found.

**Review evidence.** [Source and scope review](../../reviews/statement-promotions/2026-10-13/main-review.json) · [Promotion review index](../../reviews/statement-promotions/README.md).

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.1.7) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13

For a d-regular bipartite one-ended Borel graph G on a Polish space X, with finite d≥1, is there a G-invariant comeagre Borel set Y⊆X such that G restricted to Y has a Borel proper edge coloring using d colors? One-ended means that each connected component has exactly one end.

## Q4807. Treeability of Archimedean orders on ℤ³

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.3.2) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13

Is the orbit equivalence relation E(GL₃(ℤ) acting on Ar(ℤ³)) treeable? Ar(A) is the standard Borel space of Archimedean translation-invariant total orders on the additive group A; GL₃(ℤ) acts on Ar(ℤ³) by transporting orders. Treeable means generated by a Borel acyclic graph.

**Source.** Antoine Poulin. *A walk through the measurable arboretum*. McGill University, 2026. ## Q4808. Borel complexity of Archimedean orders over ℤ[1/n]

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.3.4) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13

For integers n≥2, determine the Borel complexity of the isomorphism relation of Archimedean orders on the additive group ℤ[1/n]², equivalently the orbit equivalence relation of GL₂(ℤ[1/n]) acting on Ar(ℤ[1/n]²). Here ℤ[1/n]={a/n^j:a∈ℤ,j≥0}, and orders are identified when a group automorphism transports one to the other.

## Q4809. Borel reducibility between ranks of Archimedean orders

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.3.5) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13

Let E_r be the orbit equivalence relation of GL_r(ℤ) on Ar(ℤ^r), the space of Archimedean orders on the additive group ℤ^r. Determine the Borel reducibility relations between these ranks: for sufficiently large integers n&lt;m, can E_m≤\_B E_n hold, and does E_n≤\_B E_m always hold? A Borel reduction f must satisfy x E y if and only if f(x) F f(y).

**Source.** Antoine Poulin. *A walk through the measurable arboretum*. McGill University, 2026. [primary source](https://mcgill.scholaris.ca/server/api/core/bitstreams/b2a18854-3768-498d-be32-2c314563b3c8/content) · [record](https://mcgill.scholaris.ca/handle/20.500.14905/141069) Location: Question 5.3.5, printed p. 97 / PDF p. 104; published paper Question 3.5. The source’s GL_r(ℤ^r) is its notation for the automorphism action on ℤ^r.

**Literature check.** Reviewed 2026-10-13: The source retains both directions as questions. Hyperfiniteness and treeability results alone do not settle the full reducibility pattern. No later answer located.

<a id="q4810"></a>

## Q4810. Amenability of ideal Bernoulli Voronoi tessellations

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.6.2) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13

Fix a countable nonamenable group Γ, a left-invariant proper metric, and a fixed measurable equivariant rule assigning nearest-center ties. Independently retain each group element as a center with probability p and partition Γ into its Voronoi cells. An ideal Bernoulli Voronoi tessellation is any subsequential weak limit, as p↓0, of the resulting invariant random equivalence relations. Must every such limit be amenable as an invariant random equivalence relation (in the source’s Definition 3.7 sense)? The unresolved target includes nonexact groups; exact groups have an affirmative answer.

**Source.** Antoine Poulin. *A walk through the measurable arboretum*. McGill University, 2026. [primary source](https://mcgill.scholaris.ca/server/api/core/bitstreams/b2a18854-3768-498d-be32-2c314563b3c8/content) · [record](https://mcgill.scholaris.ca/handle/20.500.14905/141069) Location: Question 6.6.2, printed p. 120 / PDF p. 127; construction §6.6, PDF pp. 126–127. March 27, 2026 accepted paper: §6, Question 1, arXiv:2502.11622v2; amenability Definition 3.7 there.

**Literature check.** Reviewed 2026-10-13: The March 2026 accepted version proves amenability for exact groups, produces nonamenable limits of other finite IREs on nonexact groups, and explicitly leaves this specific Voronoi question open. No later solution found.

<a id="q4811"></a>

## Q4811. Indistinguishability of ideal Bernoulli Voronoi cells

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.6.4) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13

Fix a countable nonamenable group Γ, a left-invariant proper metric, and a fixed measurable equivariant rule assigning nearest-center ties. Independently retain each group element as a center with probability p and partition Γ into its Voronoi cells. An ideal Bernoulli Voronoi tessellation is any subsequential weak limit, as p↓0, of the resulting invariant random equivalence relations. Must every such limit have indistinguishable cells? Indistinguishability means that every measurable Γ-equivariant cell selection rule is almost surely trivial (it selects all cells or no cells).

**Source.** Antoine Poulin. *A walk through the measurable arboretum*. McGill University, 2026. [primary source](https://mcgill.scholaris.ca/server/api/core/bitstreams/b2a18854-3768-498d-be32-2c314563b3c8/content) · [record](https://mcgill.scholaris.ca/handle/20.500.14905/141069) Location: First question in Question 6.6.4, printed p. 120 / PDF p. 127; cell-selection definition immediately before it. March 27, 2026 accepted paper: §6, first question in Question 3, arXiv:2502.11622v2.

**Literature check.** Reviewed 2026-10-13: The accepted paper retains this discrete Bernoulli question. The cited indistinguishability theorem concerns ideal Poisson tessellations on symmetric spaces, not all discrete groups. No closure of the exact discrete target found.

**Review evidence.** [Source and scope review](../../reviews/statement-promotions/2026-10-13/main-review.json) · [Promotion review index](../../reviews/statement-promotions/README.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.11622v2) · [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2502.11622v2#S6) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10009)

<a id="q4815"></a>

## Q4815. Reducing Archimedean-order isomorphism to a rank-three linear action

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.3.1) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13

For which integer ranks n≥2 does the orbit equivalence relation of GL_n(ℤ) acting on Ar(ℤ^n) Borel reduce to the orbit equivalence relation of GL₃(ℤ) acting by matrix multiplication on ℝ³? Ar(ℤ^n) is the standard Borel space of Archimedean translation-invariant total orders on ℤ^n. A Borel reduction f must satisfy x E y if and only if f(x) F f(y). The target is the action on ℝ³, not on Ar(ℤ³).

**Source.** Antoine Poulin. *A walk through the measurable arboretum*. McGill University, 2026. [primary source](https://mcgill.scholaris.ca/server/api/core/bitstreams/b2a18854-3768-498d-be32-2c314563b3c8/content) · [record](https://mcgill.scholaris.ca/handle/20.500.14905/141069) Location: Question 5.3.1, printed p. 97 / PDF p. 104; paper counterpart Question 3.1, arXiv:2403.11326. The literal fixed rank-three target is retained from both sources.

**Literature check.** Reviewed 2026-10-13: The paper and April 2026 thesis retain this comparison question. It differs from the within-family rank comparison because its target is a linear action on vectors rather than transported orders. No later resolution found. The source leaves n as a parameter; the promoted classification question keeps n variable and asks which ranks admit a reduction.

**Review evidence.** [Source and scope review](../../reviews/statement-promotions/2026-10-13/main-review.json) · [Promotion review index](../../reviews/statement-promotions/README.md).

## Q4816. Borel degree of isomorphism across all finitely generated Archimedean orders

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.3.3) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13

Determine the Borel complexity of the equivalence relation E on the disjoint union ⨆\_{n≥2} Ar(ℤ^n) that identifies two Archimedean ordered groups exactly when they are isomorphic as ordered groups. Within a rank n, equivalence is the orbit relation of GL_n(ℤ) acting by transport of orders; distinct ranks are inequivalent. The classification target is the Borel reducibility degree of this countable union, including how it compares to the individual rank relations.

**Source.** Antoine Poulin. *A walk through the measurable arboretum*. McGill University, 2026. [primary source](https://mcgill.scholaris.ca/server/api/core/bitstreams/b2a18854-3768-498d-be32-2c314563b3c8/content) · [record](https://mcgill.scholaris.ca/handle/20.500.14905/141069) Location: Question 5.3.3 and displayed disjoint union, printed p. 97 / PDF p. 104; paper counterpart Question 3.3, arXiv:2403.11326.

**Literature check.** Reviewed 2026-10-13: Both current source texts retain the all-ranks complexity question. Higher-rank nonhyperfinite/nontreeable results give lower bounds, not a classification of the Borel degree of the union. No later classification located.

## Q4817. Uniqueness of the low-intensity Bernoulli Voronoi limit

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.6.3) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13

Fix a countable nonamenable group Γ, a left-invariant proper metric, and a fixed measurable equivariant rule assigning nearest-center ties. Independently retain each group element as a center with probability p and partition Γ into its Voronoi cells. An ideal Bernoulli Voronoi tessellation is any subsequential weak limit, as p↓0, of the resulting invariant random equivalence relations. ## Q4894. Regressive Martin dichotomy on arithmetic degrees

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 9.5) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13-250

In ZF+AD, let f:2^ω→2^ω respect arithmetic equivalence and satisfy f(x)≤\_A x for every x. Here y≤\_A x means y is Turing reducible to some finite iterate of the Turing jump of x. Must there be an arithmetic cone on which either f(x)≡\_A a for one fixed real a, or f(x)≡\_A x? An arithmetic cone is {x:b≤\_A x} for some b.

**Source.** Patrick Lutz. *Results on Martin’s Conjecture*. University of California, Berkeley, 2021. [primary source](https://websites.umich.edu/~pglutz/thesis.pdf) · [record](https://websites.umich.edu/~pglutz/thesis.pdf) Location: Question 9.5, printed p. 131 / held PDF p. 139; definitions and conventions in Chapters 1, 4–6.

**Literature check.** Reviewed 2026-10-13: The 2025 published hyperarithmetic-regressive result settles a different reducibility. The author’s arithmetic question also remains explicit in his primary slides. Current author work and exact arithmetic-regressive retrieval supplied no full resolution.

**Review evidence.** [Source and scope review](../../reviews/statement-promotions/2026-10-13-250/root-reviews/lutz-2021.json) · [Promotion review index](../../reviews/statement-promotions/README.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2305.19646v3) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.05746) · [3](https://websites.umich.edu/~pglutz/) · [4](https://websites.umich.edu/~pglutz/questions.html) · [5](https://math.berkeley.edu/~pglutz/jmm_slides.pdf)

## Q4895. Order-preserving Martin dichotomy on hyperarithmetic degrees

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 9.6) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13-250

Under ZF+AD, if f:2^ω→2^ω satisfies x≤\_H y⇒f(x)≤\_H f(y), must some hyperarithmetic cone satisfy either f(x)≡\_H a for one real a, or x≤\_H f(x)? Here ≤\_H is hyperarithmetic reducibility and a hyperarithmetic cone is {x:b≤\_H x} for some b.

## Q4897. A nonprincipal ultrafilter strictly below Martin measure

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 9.9) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13-250

Work in ZF+AD. Let D be the Turing degrees and U_M their cone ultrafilter. For ultrafilters U,V on D, U≤\_RK V means U=F_\*(V) for some F:D→D, where A∈F_\*(V) iff F⁻¹(A)∈V. Equivalence means reducibility both ways; it does not require a bijection. Does some nonprincipal ultrafilter U on D satisfy U≤\_RK U_M but U_M≰\_RK U?

**Source.** Patrick Lutz. *Results on Martin’s Conjecture*. University of California, Berkeley, 2021. [primary source](https://websites.umich.edu/~pglutz/thesis.pdf) · [record](https://websites.umich.edu/~pglutz/thesis.pdf) Location: Question 9.9, printed p. 131 / held PDF p. 139; definitions and conventions in Chapters 1, 4–6.

**Literature check.** Reviewed 2026-10-13: The current Lutz–Siskind Question 3 retains the strict-below target. Its equivalence with one component of Martin’s conjecture gives a route, not a proof. No primary resolution was located.

**Review evidence.** [Source and scope review](../../reviews/statement-promotions/2026-10-13-250/root-reviews/lutz-2021.json) · [Promotion review index](../../reviews/statement-promotions/README.md).

## Q4898. A reasonable logic capturing polynomial time on unordered structures

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** stated as open · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13-250

Capturing Ptime means defining exactly the polynomial-time decidable isomorphism-invariant Boolean properties of finite relational structures, with no input order provided. Does there exist a logic with a decidable set of sentences and an effective compiler translating each sentence φ into an evaluation algorithm and a polynomial bounding its running time, such that every such Ptime property is defined by some sentence?

**Source.** Moritz Lichter. *Continuing the Quest for a Logic Capturing Polynomial Time – Potential, Limitations, and Interplay of Current Approaches*. Technische Universität Darmstadt, 2023. [primary source](https://tuprints.ulb.tu-darmstadt.de/server/api/core/bitstreams/a2813250-ef68-4424-8074-1d0174008f2c/content) · [record](https://tuprints.ulb.tu-darmstadt.de/server/api/core/bitstreams/a2813250-ef68-4424-8074-1d0174008f2c/content) Location: Discussion/open question, held PDF p. 15, printed p. 3; precise operators and encodings in Chapters 2, 5–7.

**Literature check.** Reviewed 2026-10-13: The held source and Grädel–Grohe primary survey leave the general Gurevich question open. Fresh retrieval of current choiceless and witnessed-choice work found restricted classes and separations of specific candidate logics, with no construction or nonexistence theorem for the full target.

**Review evidence.** [Source and scope review](../../reviews/statement-promotions/2026-10-13-250/root-reviews/lichter-2023.json) · [Promotion review index](../../reviews/statement-promotions/README.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.14003) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.12182) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2104.12999) · [4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.07869) · [5](https://logic.rwth-aachen.de/pub/graedel/cptYuri.pdf)

## Q4899. Lebesgue and Baire ultrafilters below Martin measure

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 9.13) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13-250

Work in ZF+AD. Let D be the Turing degrees and U_M their cone ultrafilter. For ultrafilters U,V on D, U≤\_RK V means U=F_\*(V) for some F:D→D, where A∈F_\*(V) iff F⁻¹(A)∈V. Equivalence means reducibility both ways; it does not require a bijection. Let U_L consist of subsets A⊂D whose inverse image under x↦deg_T(x) has fair-coin measure one, and let U_B consist of those whose inverse image is comeager. Do U_L≤\_RK U_M and U_B≤\_RK U_M hold?

**Source.** Patrick Lutz. *Results on Martin’s Conjecture*. University of California, Berkeley, 2021. [primary source](https://websites.umich.edu/~pglutz/thesis.pdf) · [record](https://websites.umich.edu/~pglutz/thesis.pdf) Location: Question 9.13, printed p. 132 / held PDF p. 140; definitions and conventions in Chapters 1, 4–6.

**Literature check.** Reviewed 2026-10-13: Successor Question 5 still asks both comparisons. The proved results that U_M is not below U_L or U_B are the reverse direction and leave the proposed comparisons open.

## Q4900. Rank-logic queries are definable in Choiceless Polynomial Time

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** stated as open · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13-250

Rank logic is inflationary fixed-point logic with counting and the uniform finite-field rank operator of Chapter 7, whose prime characteristic can be specified numerically in the formula. CPT is the source’s Choiceless Polynomial Time logic with counting, using hereditarily finite sets over the input atoms with polynomial bounds on running time and the number of active sets, and no arbitrary choice of an atom. Is every rank-logic-definable Boolean property of finite relational structures definable in CPT?

**Source.** Moritz Lichter. *Continuing the Quest for a Logic Capturing Polynomial Time – Potential, Limitations, and Interplay of Current Approaches*. Technische Universität Darmstadt, 2023. [primary source](https://tuprints.ulb.tu-darmstadt.de/server/api/core/bitstreams/a2813250-ef68-4424-8074-1d0174008f2c/content) · [record](https://tuprints.ulb.tu-darmstadt.de/server/api/core/bitstreams/a2813250-ef68-4424-8074-1d0174008f2c/content) Location: Discussion/open question, held PDF p. 22, printed p. 10; precise operators and encodings in Chapters 2, 5–7.

**Literature check.** Reviewed 2026-10-13: The source proves that CPT is not contained in rank logic, which is the reverse inclusion. Its diagram leaves rank logic contained in CPT unresolved. Current primary rank/CPT retrieval yielded no exact proof or counterexample for this direction.

**Review evidence.** [Source and scope review](../../reviews/statement-promotions/2026-10-13-250/root-reviews/lichter-2023.json) · [Promotion review index](../../reviews/statement-promotions/README.md).

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** stated as open · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13-250

Capturing Ptime means defining exactly the polynomial-time decidable isomorphism-invariant Boolean properties of finite relational structures, with no input order provided. ## Q4964. Two minimal normal measures from local supercompactness

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.1.1) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13-250

Work in ZFC. A normal measure on κ is a nonprincipal κ-complete ultrafilter U on κ such that every regressive function on a member of U is constant on a member of U. For cardinals κ≤λ, κ is λ-supercompact if there is an elementary embedding j:V→M into a transitive inner model with critical point κ, j(κ)>λ and j[λ]∈M. It is supercompact if this holds for every λ≥κ. If κ is 2^κ-supercompact, must there be at least two distinct normal measures U on κ containing {α<κ:α is not a measurable cardinal}?

**Source.** Gabriel Goldberg. *The Ultrapower Axiom*. Harvard University, 2019. [primary source](https://dash.harvard.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/bfd6b6d3-6048-4a24-b83f-1741414a1447/content) · [record](https://dash.harvard.edu/handle/1/42029483) Location: Question 2.1.1, printed p. 7 / held PDF p. 14.

**Literature check.** Reviewed 2026-10-13: UA makes the normal-measure Mitchell order linear; consequently every measurable κ has exactly one normal measure concentrating on nonmeasurables (thesis§2.1 and Theorem2.3.11). Thus UA plus a2^κ-supercompact κ supplies a conditional negative instance. The checked results do not establish consistency of that UA/large-cardinal combination; no unconditional ZFC resolution was located.

**Review evidence.** [Source and scope review](../../reviews/statement-promotions/2026-10-13-250/root-reviews/goldberg-2019.json) · [Promotion review index](../../reviews/statement-promotions/README.md).

<a id="q4965"></a>

## Q4965. More than one normal measure on every strongly compact cardinal

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.1.2) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13-250

Work in ZFC. A normal measure on κ is a nonprincipal κ-complete ultrafilter U on κ such that every regressive function on a member of U is constant on a member of U. Put P_κ(λ)={a⊂λ:|a|<κ}. A fine ultrafilter on this set contains {a:ξ∈a} for every ξ<λ. An uncountable cardinal κ is λ-strongly compact if P_κ(λ) carries a κ-complete fine ultrafilter; κ is strongly compact if this holds for every λ≥κ. Must every strongly compact cardinal κ carry at least two distinct normal measures?

**Source.** Gabriel Goldberg. *The Ultrapower Axiom*. Harvard University, 2019. [primary source](https://dash.harvard.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/bfd6b6d3-6048-4a24-b83f-1741414a1447/content) · [record](https://dash.harvard.edu/handle/1/42029483) Location: Question 2.1.2, printed p. 7 / held PDF p. 14.

**Literature check.** Reviewed 2026-10-13: Gitik–Kaplan§4 construct a strongly compact least measurable carrying exactly one normal measure, starting from a supercompact with a unique nonmeasurable-concentrating measure. Corollary4.3 prepares2^κ=κ⁺; Theorems4.4/4.5 require no inaccessible aboveκ. They describe a negative answer from a supercompact plus UA. This is a conditional countermodel construction, without a known consistency proof of that initial UA/large-cardinal combination.

**Review evidence.** [Source and scope review](../../reviews/statement-promotions/2026-10-13-250/root-reviews/goldberg-2019.json) · [Promotion review index](../../reviews/statement-promotions/README.md).

## Q4966. Extendibility with unique minimal normal measures

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.1.3) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13-250

A normal measure on κ is a nonprincipal κ-complete ultrafilter U on κ such that every regressive function on a member of U is constant on a member of U. A cardinal δ is extendible if for every α>δ there are β and an elementary embedding e:V_α→V_β with critical point δ and e(δ)>α. Is ZFC consistent with an extendible cardinal and the assertion that every measurable cardinal κ carries exactly one normal measure containing {α<κ:α is not measurable}?

**Source.** Gabriel Goldberg. *The Ultrapower Axiom*. Harvard University, 2019. [primary source](https://dash.harvard.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/bfd6b6d3-6048-4a24-b83f-1741414a1447/content) · [record](https://dash.harvard.edu/handle/1/42029483) Location: Conjecture 2.1.3, printed p. 8 / held PDF p. 15.

**Literature check.** Reviewed 2026-10-13: The author’s2025 determinacy paper explicitly leaves canonical models at strong compactness and supercompactness unknown; its determinacy UA theorem does not construct a model with an extendible cardinal. No resolution of this consistency target was located in the bounded current-primary scan.

Work in ZFC. The Ultrapower Axiom (UA) says that any two well-founded ultrapower embeddings jᵢ:V→Mᵢ by countably complete ultrafilters admit internal ultrapower embeddings kᵢ:Mᵢ→N, using ultrafilters belonging to Mᵢ, with k₀∘j₀=k₁∘j₁. ## Q4972. Local strong compactness extends the club filter

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 8.3.19 (ZFC)) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13-250

Work in ZFC. Put P_κ(λ)={a⊂λ:|a|<κ}. A fine ultrafilter on this set contains {a:ξ∈a} for every ξ<λ. An uncountable cardinal κ is λ-strongly compact if P_κ(λ) carries a κ-complete fine ultrafilter; κ is strongly compact if this holds for every λ≥κ. Let λ≥κ be regular. If κ is λ-strongly compact, must there be a κ-complete ultrafilter on P_κ(λ) containing every club subset? Here a club is unbounded under inclusion and closed under unions of increasing chains of nonzero length less than κ.

## Q4976. The local UA inner-model hypothesis above an extendible cardinal

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 8.4.32 (UA Conjecture)) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13-250

Work in ZFC. A cardinal δ is extendible if for every α>δ there are β and an elementary embedding e:V_α→V_β with critical point δ and e(δ)>α. Work in ZFC. The Ultrapower Axiom (UA) says that any two well-founded ultrapower embeddings jᵢ:V→Mᵢ by countably complete ultrafilters admit internal ultrapower embeddings kᵢ:Mᵢ→N, using ultrafilters belonging to Mᵢ, with k₀∘j₀=k₁∘j₁. Must every extendible cardinal δ admit an inner model N satisfying UA with the δ-cover and δ-approximation properties? Here δ-cover means that for every ordinal θ and A⊂θ of V-size less than δ there is B∈N with A⊂B⊂θ and |B|^V<δ; δ-approximation means that any A⊂θ whose intersections with every B∈N of V-size less than δ belong to N must itself belong to N.

**Source.** Gabriel Goldberg. *The Ultrapower Axiom*. Harvard University, 2019. [primary source](https://dash.harvard.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/bfd6b6d3-6048-4a24-b83f-1741414a1447/content) · [record](https://dash.harvard.edu/handle/1/42029483) Location: Conjecture 8.4.32 (UA Conjecture), printed p. 402 / held PDF p. 409.

**Literature check.** Reviewed 2026-10-13: The current primary determinacy results establish UA in specified HOD models; they do not establish this δ-cover/approximation inner model for every extendible δ in an arbitrary ZFC universe. No exact resolution was located.

**Review evidence.** [Source and scope review](../../reviews/statement-promotions/2026-10-13-250/root-reviews/goldberg-2019.json) · [Promotion review index](../../reviews/statement-promotions/README.md).

<a id="q4977"></a>

## Q4977. Pathology at a successor of a singular cardinal

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 8.4.38 (ZFC)) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13-250

Work in ZFC. A cardinal λ is pathological in the source sense if there is an elementary embedding j:V→M into a transitive inner model such that j[μ]∈M for every cardinal μ<λ, some A∈M contains j[λ] and has M-cardinality at most λ, but j[λ]∉M. Can λ=μ⁺ be pathological when μ is a singular cardinal?

**Source.** Gabriel Goldberg. *The Ultrapower Axiom*. Harvard University, 2019. [primary source](https://dash.harvard.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/bfd6b6d3-6048-4a24-b83f-1741414a1447/content) · [record](https://dash.harvard.edu/handle/1/42029483) Location: Question 8.4.38 (ZFC), printed p. 406 / held PDF p. 413.

**Literature check.** Reviewed 2026-10-13: The held source defines this precise pathology and retains the question after its partial dichotomies. Current primary embedding/UA works checked did not resolve this exact target; no full closure was located in the bounded dated scan.

**Review evidence.** [Source and scope review](../../reviews/statement-promotions/2026-10-13-250/root-reviews/goldberg-2019.json) · [Promotion review index](../../reviews/statement-promotions/README.md).

<a id="q4978"></a>

## Q4978. Regular pathological cardinals compatible with UA

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 8.4.40) · **Reviewed extraction** 2026-10-13-250

Work in ZFC. The Ultrapower Axiom (UA) says that any two well-founded ultrapower embeddings jᵢ:V→Mᵢ by countably complete ultrafilters admit internal ultrapower embeddings kᵢ:Mᵢ→N, using ultrafilters belonging to Mᵢ, with k₀∘j₀=k₁∘j₁. A cardinal λ is pathological in the source sense if there is an elementary embedding j:V→M into a transitive inner model such that j[μ]∈M for every cardinal μ<λ, some A∈M contains j[λ] and has M-cardinality at most λ, but j[λ]∉M. Is ZFC+UA consistent with the existence of a regular pathological cardinal λ?

**Source.** Gabriel Goldberg. *The Ultrapower Axiom*. Harvard University, 2019. [primary source](https://dash.harvard.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/bfd6b6d3-6048-4a24-b83f-1741414a1447/content) · [record](https://dash.harvard.edu/handle/1/42029483) Location: Conjecture 8.4.40, printed p. 406 / held PDF p. 413.

**Literature check.** Reviewed 2026-10-13: The held source defines this precise pathology and retains the question after its partial dichotomies. Current primary embedding/UA works checked did not resolve this exact target; no full closure was located in the bounded dated scan.
